§1 — What a coordinator had to do by hand, and why none of it needed a coordinator

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T14:18:48.562Z

reuse map for sections 3, 4 and 5: _write_shadow_patch (promotion.py:1175) plus run_dir (runs.py:69) are the durable-artifact shape section 3 needs, synthesize_landed_record (evidence.py:244) already composes the record section 4 hand-authors, and _worker_seconds (ledger.py:1959) plus concurrency_bounds (summary.py:397) already hold section 5's arithmetic

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T17:55:00+00:00

A tenth item in this plan's shape, measured four times in one session rather than reported from another one. A worker process ends mid-turn, having done substantial work, without writing a terminal manifest or its deliverable. The surface knows: the pointer's process is dead, the manifest is non-terminal, and a resumable session id is recorded against the member. The coordinator then runs crew resume --advice by hand, and writes the advice slightly differently each time — which is the signature this plan uses to identify work that fell through a gap.

The four, with what was at stake in each. A review that had accumulated 2,922 stream records and written nothing; the wall-time node at 12,625 records with three commits already made and a green gate on disk, needing only the manifest that would let it be promoted; and two further reviews, one of which had to be resumed twice over. In every case the work existed and only the delivery was missing, so a redispatch would have discarded it — which is exactly why the recovery has to be resume and exactly why it cannot be left to whoever happens to notice.

Why it is a gap rather than ordinary operation. crew resume-ready already sweeps and resumes, but by its own contract only for runs whose provider hold or declared external wait has ended. A run whose process simply stopped is not in that set, though it is the more common case here and the one the classifier already labels. The distance between what the classifier knows and what the sweep acts on is the whole item.

One caution for whoever cuts the node. Automatic resumption must not re-enter a worker that is merely slow, so the condition is a dead process with a non-terminal manifest and a captured session, not an idle stream — and the advice it supplies should be the one thing every one of these four needed, which is to write the deliverable and the manifest before doing anything else.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T17:45:00+00:00

An eleventh item in this plan's shape, measured twice today. The review reflex fires on a run's terminal transition and does not know the coordinator has resumed that run. Both times, a node reached unpromoted, the reflex dispatched a review, and within about ninety seconds I resumed the worker with a correction — so a reviewer spent its budget reading a tree being rewritten beneath it, and its verdict would have been stale on arrival with nothing marking it so.

The surface holds both facts. The run's pointer records that it was resumed and that its process is alive again; the review's node records which run it targets. Nothing joins them. Both times the race was caught only because a coordinator happened to be watching transitions closely, and the remedy was to stop and discard a worker that had done nothing wrong.

It compounds with the display. A discarded run renders in the ticker as stopped → promoted, so the tidy-up reads as a landing. Three times today a run I stopped deliberately was reported as promoted, and only the committed ledger distinguished them.

The repair is a check, not a rule: a review dispatch names the run it reviews, so refuse or defer it when that run's process is alive, and re-dispatch when the run next reaches terminal. Nothing else needs to know about resumes.

§2 — A roster write that rides someone else's commit

unknown · undated

The transcript settles which of the two repairs removes the work, and it is not the smaller one.

Dispatch refused each unknown member with identical text: project 'imas-efit' has no crew member 'clive-47'; register it with reckon crew member add before dispatching to it. The coordinator registered in batches by shell loop — five to nine members at a time, then several singly — always ahead of the dispatch that needed them once the refusal had taught them to. So the shape of the work was: one refusal per new node the first time, then a pre-registration loop per wave, then one chore(crew) commit per batch.

Their conclusion, which the transcript supports: the repair that removes all of it is dispatch registering an unknown member itself, with the harness and role it was already given. A range form on member add would only shorten the loop — it would make the busy work faster rather than absent, which is the distinction §1 is built on.

Recorded as evidence for the member-registration-owner decision rather than as its resolution; the choice remains the lead's.

unknown · undated

The clearest instance of this plan's subject: a coordinator edited a crew JSON file by hand, because no command in the surface can do what was needed.

A node had declared reckon/crew/recovery.py as an exclusive write path and then proved the classifier already correct, changing no source. Its manifest recorded the file untouched and omitted it from changed_paths. Another session was blocked on that path. The claim was real, held by a terminal run, and describing work that never happened.

There is no way to narrow it. The crew surface carries twenty-three commands and --write-path appears on dispatch alone — a claim can be created and never amended. The two available moves are both wrong: discard removes the pointer and takes the resume path with it, and doing nothing blocks a peer indefinitely. So this coordinator opened ~/.config/reckon/crew/live/<run>.json, filtered the list, and wrote it back. A peer session independently endorsed that as the supported remedy, which is the tell that the gap is structural rather than a lapse.

Hand-editing it went wrong on the first attempt, in the way hand-editing does. The filter was a substring test, so 'recovery.py' not in path also dropped tests/test_crew_recovery.py — a file the node genuinely had changed. Caught on the readback and restored from a copy taken beforehand. A command would have matched on equality and never offered that mistake.

The surface that is missing narrows a live run's declared write paths, takes the manifest as its evidence, and refuses when the manifest still names the path as changed. That last clause is what separates it from an override: it is not a coordinator overruling the fence, it is the record correcting a declaration the work did not use.

The lead's statement of the requirement is the plainest form of this plan's thesis: orchestrators should not be editing crew JSON files because the tools do not provide the correct surface.

A ledger write that would reduce the roster without being asked to is now refused, and the one caller entitled to a removal states it. The guard compares the member id set of the incoming write against the ledger version it replaces and refuses when an id the stored roster holds is left out, naming the dropped ids and the flag that permits a genuine removal (allow_member_removal=True, keyword-only so the intent is visible at the call site). Identity is guarded rather than count: a same-count swap loses an id while the count reports nothing missing, so it is refused too. The roster check answers to the version check rather than pre-empting it, so a write prepared against a stale version is refused for that reason and the caller's re-read meets the guard on a current roster.

The brief stated that all four ledger.write callers preserve members. One does not: reap idle-members filters the roster down to the surviving rows and writes it back, a deliberate removal and the reaper's whole purpose. That call site sat outside the roster-guard claim and was granted as a fence extension; it is now the single caller passing allow_member_removal=True, with the reap named as the intended removal, and every other caller passes nothing and has its unrequested decrease refused. Reproduction in both directions with one idle session member in a temp checkout and temp configuration home: at base the reap retired it; with the guard carrying no intent flag the reap raised a dispatch failure after twelve attempts and the roster was unchanged; with the flag at that call site the reap retires the idle member and leaves the active one in place.

crew-worker · 2026-09-17

A run's write claim can now be narrowed or released through a module rather than by hand-editing its live pointer. The gap c-no-command-narrows-a-claim records is closed at the mechanism; the command that exposes it is left to the caller, because reckon/cli.py is owned by a second node.

The gap was re-established from the surface rather than quoted. Across all 31 crew subcommands, --write-path appears in dispatch help alone — positive control: the same scan finds it there — and no crew claim subcommand exists. At base 6c577fa2, git cat-file -e 6c577fa2:reckon/crew/claims.py exits 128, so no release path existed in any form.

Commit c754cfe2 adds reckon/crew/claims.py: release_claim(run_id, paths=None, reason=…) and narrow_claim(run_id, keep=…, reason=…) rewrite the pointer's node.write_paths and return a ClaimAmendment report carrying the previous and current declarations, the paths removed, and any unintegrated work still held by the stopped run. Refusal, not override, is the whole of the safety. An amendment is admitted only for a worker proven stopped on this host: a live process refuses, because releasing a running worker's claim is how two workers end up writing one file; a pointer whose launcher_host is not this host refuses, because a pid answers only on the machine that issued it and the crew config home is shared across login nodes, so a foreign answer would be fabricated; and a pointer that records no process yet refuses, because it is written before its worker is spawned and that window is what a second misplaced writer waits for. Refusing costs a wait; admitting costs someone's worker.

Verified through the dispatch-equivalent claim check, not by reading the pointer back. Ten tests, each asserting what plan_scope_lanes derives for a candidate declaring the path, which is the admission path itself. The falsifier fails at base by construction and the range covers the shapes that differ in kind: release while the run is terminal; narrowing two paths to one; and a release attempted against a live process, which refuses and leaves the claim intact. Refusals also cover a foreign launching host, a pointer with no process, a path the run never declared, and a narrowing that drops nothing; positive controls confirm an untouched claim still refuses a second writer, and that removing the pointer releases the claim exactly as promotion does. Fixtures build a synthetic repository and a temporary crew home, and the fixture asserts the real crew config home is byte-identical afterwards, since this module writes there.

Gate, judged as a delta. The four-file cohort was run on all_debug, and the heavy member was attributed by running the same tree at base: tests/test_crew.py reports 11 failed, 193 passed at head and the same 11 identifiers, 193 passed at base, so the node added zero failures. The remaining cohort carries 10 pre-existing failures, all in test_crew_cross_project_claims.py; the change is additive, and git diff --stat 6c577fa2..HEAD is two new files with no existing file modified.

The command surface the caller should add, deliberately not written here: reckon crew claim narrow --run <id> --keep P… --reason TEXT and reckon crew claim release --run <id> [--path P…] --reason TEXT, rendering ClaimAmendment.as_dict(). Full receipts: claim release evidence.

crew-worker · 2026-09-18

A dispatch now registers and commits the session member it provisions for itself, so the row reaches the repository instead of the checkout that wrote it. The §2 defect reproduced at base 7dfae503: a session registration left docs/state/<project>/crew.json untracked and made no commit, so the row was visible only to the checkout that wrote it and rode whichever commit came next.

Commit 6a0bb500 requests the commit where the write happens — _register_session_member passes commit=True to ledger.register_member — so the registration lands as chore(roster): register <id> carrying only the roster file. A commit that fails is surfaced as a CrewError naming the uncommitted registration rather than retried into a registration the caller believes landed.

The committing registration exposed an ordering defect in the same path, and it is the reason this node changed a second function. A dispatch reaps idle session rows before it registers its own, so the two roster writes land in one checkout in sequence. A reap left in the tree is content that is not the registration's to record, so the registration's commit must refuse it — and then the dispatch provisions no member at all. The reaper now commits the removal it writes, and skips that commit when the roster already carries content that is not its own, which leaves the write in the tree exactly as an unrequested write would and leaves the next named commit to refuse it.

Four-file gate at base and at the repair: base 10 failed, 19 passed (the seven registration falsifiers plus three pre-existing test_crew_members.py failures), repair 3 failed, 26 passed with the same three pre-existing ids — zero added failures and 7 falsifiers fixed. The three remaining failures raise spec-level: no specification level is declared from reckon/crew/dispatch.py:2787 and fail identically at base; they are outside this node's write scope. Regression coverage: tests/test_member_registration_commits.py (nine tests, each a synthesised temporary repository and temporary crew home, asserting the live home and the shared checkout are untouched). Full record: dispatch registers and commits evidence.

This discharges the paragraph above headed “What this does not settle”. That paragraph records dispatch-registers as unbuilt; it is built here.

§3 — A cited gate log that will not be there later

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T14:35:00+00:00

A third instance, measured in this session, of the compensation this section names. The investigation node for §6 read its positive control from stdout and kept no file, so its cited check would have resolved to nothing. Promotion correctly refused a passing gate with no evidence, and the only remedy available was for the coordinator to re-run the check and write the log into the run directory by hand before citing it — which is what happened.

The section's premise also still holds at the merged head: reckon/crew/promotion.py contains zero copy operations today, so --gate-log-path continues to record where a log lies rather than preserving it. The count of coordinators independently inventing this compensation is now three, across three sessions.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T16:30:00+00:00

Correction to a claim this plan carries. The open followup states that a review run cannot be reconciled at all, so the unreconcilable backlog accrues one per node reviewed. Tested directly today and it is not true: crew complete --no-commit with gate evidence reconciled a review run cleanly, its pointer is gone and its record sits in the committed ledger. Acting on the stale claim is itself what grows the backlog, because a coordinator who believes reviews cannot be reconciled does not try.

What is true is narrower and belongs to §3. A review run has no gate of its own and nothing produces evidence for it, so the promotion contract's evidence requirement can only be met by the coordinator writing the check and its log by hand. That is what happened here: a five-line script confirming the record parses, carries five dimensions and scores them in range, captured into the run directory purely so the row could cite something durable.

Running total for this session alone: the coordinator hand-produced a gate log four times — twice for investigate nodes whose positive controls printed to stdout, once for this review, and once re-running a check after a worker kept no file. Every one is the compensation this section exists to remove, and none of them needed a coordinator.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T20:55:00+00:00

The gate-evidence guard contradicts itself on the same mistake, and I made that mistake twice tonight so it is measured rather than reasoned. Both times I cited a node's negative-control log as the gate log for a passing gate — a log of a deliberately reddened run offered as proof of green.

cited log ends withguard
4 failed, 6 passed in 0.82s then EXIT=1refused — "the log contradicts the asserted status"
3 failed, 6 passed in 1.22s, no EXIT= lineaccepted

So the guard reads a marker, not the log. It compares the asserted exit status against an explicit EXIT= line and has no opinion on a pytest summary that says three tests failed. The fact it needs is on the last line of the file it already opened.

The consequence is a committed row whose evidence refutes its own verdict. gate_check on the second run reads exit_status: 0 beside a log_path resolving to 3 failed, 6 passed. Every check a later reader runs on that row passes, which is the same defect this section names one layer along: the citation is durable and wrong instead of durable and absent. There is also no amend path — crew complete is one-shot, so a mis-cited row cannot be corrected, only annotated. I ran crew verify-gate, which re-ran the real command at the merged revision and patched integrated_gate_check with exit_status: 0 and integrated_verdict: passed, so the row now carries correct evidence beside the wrong citation rather than instead of it.

Two repairs, and the first is nearly free. Read the cited log's trailing summary rather than only an EXIT= marker — a line matching N failed contradicts a passing gate as surely as EXIT=1 does. Second, and larger: let a promoted row's gate citation be corrected, because a one-shot write of evidence that can be wrong is a record nobody can repair.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T14:18:52.654Z

independent review of the agreement-test retarget: 98/100, one finding noting the four retargeted cases are coupled to the copy step and red in any tree lacking it; satisfied by the merge order, with both commits now ancestors of HEAD and the file 17 passed at the merged head

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T13:43:50.445Z

the four gate-agreement assertions cite the recorded copy rather than the caller-supplied path, leaving the unreadable-citation case untouched; review 98/100, and the pair with the preservation change is green at the merged head with zero added failure ids against the stated base

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T14:24:27.830Z

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T15:06:34.582Z

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T16:00:00+00:00

§3 closes. A promotion copies its cited gate log into the run directory and records the copy, so the row cites something that outlives the worktree and the reaper. Zero added failure ids at the merged head against the stated base, measured with the retarget node merged alongside, because neither half could establish it alone.

The carve-out matters as much as the copy. My done-when required that an absent log refuse the promotion. The codebase had already decided the opposite and written the reason into a test comment: promotion may run from a machine the worker's log never reached, so a citation that cannot be read has no text to contradict the verdict and must not turn a passing promotion into a refusal. That rule won. The node was corrected to leave an unreadable citation exactly as given, and the four agreement assertions were retargeted onto the copy in a node of their own because the file lay outside this one's fence.

Three reviews, three findings, all from outside the gate. Two assertions compared the row's recorded value against the variable the test itself supplied, so they held under the very mutation meant to falsify them; the manifest claimed the declaration sat verbatim on the red log's first line and it did not; and the landed summary linked to an evidence anchor that existed in neither document. That last one is this section's own subject turned on itself — a citation resolving to nothing, on the section built to stop citations resolving to nothing. Every link in the plan now resolves to a real anchor, checked at the merged head.

One imprecision left standing deliberately: after_suite calls fe1bf820 this node's head while the commits line lists db4e9a58, because the docs-only evidence commit landed after the suite ran. The measurement is of the code state and is unaffected; the label is imprecise. Recorded rather than spending a fourth worker on it.

review-of-the-agreement-tests-assert-the-recorded-copy — delivered report

Cited from /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260922T134354086861-review-of-the-agreement-tests-assert-the-recorded-copy/verify-review-record.py.

"""Delivery gate: the stored review parses and scores every dimension in range."""
from reckon.crew.review import (
    REVIEW_DIMENSIONS, REVIEW_MAX_SCORE, ledger_block, parse_review, read_review,
)

REVIEWED = "r-20260922T132151082793-the-agreement-tests-assert-the-recorded-copy"

stored = read_review("reckon", REVIEWED)
assert stored is not None, "no stored review record"
assert stored["status"] == "parsed", stored["status"]
scores = stored["scores"]
assert set(scores) == set(REVIEW_DIMENSIONS), (sorted(scores), REVIEW_DIMENSIONS)
assert stored["absent"] == [], stored["absent"]
for dim, value in scores.items():
    assert isinstance(value, int) and 0 <= value <= REVIEW_MAX_SCORE, (dim, value)
reparsed = parse_review(stored["raw_text"])
assert reparsed["scores"] == scores, (reparsed["scores"], scores)
print("dimensions scored:", len(scores), "of", len(REVIEW_DIMENSIONS))
print("scores:", {k: scores[k] for k in REVIEW_DIMENSIONS}, "total:", stored["total"])
print("item verdicts:", stored["item_aggregate"], "absent items:", stored["absent_items"])
print("findings:", len(stored["findings"]), "call_sites:", stored.get("call_sites"))
print("reviewed_revision:", stored.get("reviewed_revision"))
print("ledger_block:", ledger_block(stored))
print("VERIFY_OK", True)

Measured on the record actually authored: about 80% composition, one paragraph of judgement the run did not hold.

Transcribed from the run's own report — the measured ratios, the plan targets, the ctest command and its result, a cache caveat and an axis-offset observation. Supplied by the coordinator and not present in the run — one paragraph attributing why the failures closed, naming three intervening landings rather than the plan's own two options, and the decision that the plan ships on its measure rather than being marked superseded.

That makes the repair precise, and it is not full automation. The coordinator's own phrasing is the design: a composed record with an empty mechanism slot the coordinator fills. Compose the 80% from the run, and leave a named slot for the attribution and the shipping decision, which are judgement and belong to a coordinator.

It also disposes of the hazard §4 raised. A record composed from the run is not a coordinator writing evidence for work it is judging; a coordinator adding an attribution paragraph into a named slot, beside composed measurements it did not author, is a different and defensible act.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T20:10:00Z

The filter-order case could not fail, and it was the case the wiring record leans on. It asserted that a filtered read reports the fleet's width by selecting plan="alpha", whose two rows were dispatched at the same instant and therefore covered each other. The fleet counted 2 at that instant and the selection counted 2 as well, so a read that filtered before counting passed the case written to exclude it.

The fixture now separates the orders, and the case says so itself. The row sharing the selected row's dispatch instant holds another plan, so the fleet reports 2 where a filter-first read would report 1. The case asserts the fleet's figure and derives the selection's own count from the same rows, so it reddens for the wrong order and equally for a fixture edit that removes the difference. A probe reading the fixture both ways records early: pre-filter=2 post-filter=1 surface=2. The declared mutation — narrowing the rows by plan and then recounting the width over that selection — reddens exactly this case (1 failed, 8 passed, assert 1 == 2); the other eight read unfiltered and never see it. reckon/ledger.py was restored byte-identically and no product file changed.

Recorded, not judged. Whether §5's width claim is now adequately evidenced at the merged head is the coordinator's call; what is established here is that the case can tell the two orders apart, which it could not before. Full record: the filter-order evidence.

§6 — A gate that measures the node rather than the fleet

unknown · undated

The gap is now specified rather than suspected, and it is half a capability rather than a missing one.

The reporting coordinator corrected their own claim before I asked: they never ran verify-gate and had it fail. They read crew --help, saw "re-run one run's gate at the integrated revision", and judged from the description. An unexecuted search, stated as such.

Checked here. verify-gate reads the run's stored gate command from its committed ledger row and re-runs that command at --revision against --checkout-path, patching the report back onto the row. Its options are project, run, checkout-path, revision, timeout. There is no suite label and no baseline revision.

So it already owns the harder half — re-running at the merged head and recording the verdict against the run rather than only printing it. What it cannot do is run something wider than the node's own gate, or compare against a baseline. Those are exactly the two things the hand-check needed.

What the hand-check was, five times, each as one srun job on a debug partition against the main checkout at the merged head: an SDCC build; a fast preset configured, built and run under the UNIT label; a VALIDATION ctest run; and the sorted failing-rail names compared by comm -13 against a baseline built the same way in a fresh detached worktree at the pre-merge head. It caught a compile error no per-node gate could see — a symbol declared only under a test macro — because a node's gate measures its own diff and an integration break is a property of the merged head. Logs under reports/ship-s13/gates/.

The coordinator's own summary is the section's requirement: if verify-gate can take a suite label and a baseline revision rather than the run's own gate command, that is the whole hand-check.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T14:23:50.698Z

verdict missing-option, verified independently: verify-gate declares six options (project, run, checkout-path, revision, timeout-seconds, pretty) and none supplies a command, which is read from the committed row at promotion.py:608 and executed as sh -c with the checkout as cwd, so running a whole-repository suite at the merged head needs one new option rather than a new surface

reckon-ship · 2026-09-21T14:35:00+00:00

Verdict: missing option, and the section's caution was justified. This section was authored as an investigation because the reporting coordinator said they could not find the option rather than that it is absent. They were right, and so was the caution. Verified independently at the merged head, not taken from the report: reckon crew verify-gate declares exactly six options — --project, --run, --checkout-path, --revision, --timeout-seconds, --pretty — and not one of them supplies a command.

Where the command actually comes from. reckon/crew/promotion.py:608, inside rerun_gate_at_integrated_revision (:574): command = str((gate_check or {}).get("command") or "").strip(), read from the run's committed ledger row and executed as sh -c with the checkout as working directory (:645–:652). Nothing downstream depends on the command's identity, which is why this is one option and not a new surface.

Folded, not handed off: the repair is a node on this section — one option on the subcommand supplying the command, declared in reckon/cli.py and taking effect at the read in reckon/crew/promotion.py:608. It is not dispatched yet because both files are under live claims, one held by a concurrent node on this plan and one by a peer session.

Scope decision recorded here rather than left open. The hand-check that motivated this section had two halves: run the fleet suite at the merged head, and compare it against a fresh baseline worktree. Only the first is this section's. The second compares two trees rather than one and its reusable parts already exist as the suite-observation and new-failure-set-difference helpers at reckon/ledger.py:235–:310, which the promotion contract already consumes through baseline_suite and after_suite. It gets no surface of its own here.

crew-worker · 2026-09-22

The missing option exists now, and it was one option rather than a new surface. crew verify-gate gained --command, which runs the given command in place of the run's stored gate command at the integrated revision, so a coordinator can measure a whole-repository suite at the merged head — or measure a run that stored no gate command at all — without a new verb. Without the option the stored command runs exactly as before.

The selection happens where the command is read from the ledger row, at promotion.py:672: command = supplied or stored_command. The supplied command wins only when non-empty, so an absent option is inert. The re-run report carries gate_command — the command that actually executed — and gate_command_source, option or stored, so a reader can tell a supplied command from the recorded one when they differ.

Falsified rather than asserted. The declared mutation replaces the selection with command = stored_command, so the option is ignored; it reddens exactly the three cases that exercise it and leaves the stored-command case green. The mutation is stated verbatim on the first line of negative-control.log and the source was restored byte-identically (empty diff).

Zero added failure ids against the named base. The same three cohort files ran at base 37de7907 in a detached worktree with the same five failure ids — test_promotion_audits_only_its_own_worktree, two crew-verb documentation contracts, and two reckon-ship skill-budget contracts — none touching verify-gate or its option. Those five are pre-existing at base and outside this node's declared scope, reported under follow-ons rather than triaged.

Scope held to the first half. The hand-check that motivated this section had two halves; only the fleet-suite-at-merged-head half is here. The baseline comparison already exists as the ledger's suite-observation and new-failure-set helpers, which promotion consumes through baseline_suite and after_suite, and this node adds no surface for it. Full record: verify-gate command selection. Commit e4209d1a.

A worker does not close its own node: this section's driving followup is left for the coordinator, who alone observes the concurrent nodes.

crew-worker · 2026-09-22

The missing option exists now, and it was one option rather than a new surface. crew verify-gate gained --command, which runs the given command in place of the run's stored gate command at the integrated revision, so a coordinator can measure a whole-repository suite at the merged head — or measure a run that stored no gate command at all — without a new verb. Without the option the stored command runs exactly as before.

The selection happens where the command is read from the ledger row, at promotion.py:672: command = supplied or stored_command. The supplied command wins only when non-empty, so an absent option is inert. The re-run report carries gate_command — the command that actually executed — and gate_command_source, option or stored, so a reader can tell a supplied command from the recorded one when they differ.

whether-verify-gate-can-run-a-whole-repository-suite — delivered report

Cited from /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reports/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/merged-head-gate.md.

missing-option

# verify-gate and the whole-repository suite

Source read at revision `5cd812f6`; no repository file was changed. This report
is the node's sole deliverable. Verifying the merged result of any repair
belongs to a separately dispatched test node.

## Where verify-gate chooses the command it runs

`reckon/crew/promotion.py:608`, inside `rerun_gate_at_integrated_revision`
(defined at `reckon/crew/promotion.py:574`), is the single expression that
chooses the command verify-gate runs. Quoted verbatim:

```python
    command = str((gate_check or {}).get("command") or "").strip()
```

The command's source is the run's committed ledger row, read by the production
caller `record_gate_rerun_at_integrated_revision` (defined at
`reckon/crew/promotion.py:677`; its only call site is `reckon/cli.py:2988`).
Quoted verbatim (`reckon/crew/promotion.py:720`):

```python
    stored_gate_check = row.get("gate_check")
```

The execution, quoted verbatim (`reckon/crew/promotion.py:645-652`):

```python
            result = subprocess.run(
                ["sh", "-c", command],
                cwd=str(repository),
                capture_output=True,
                text=True,
                check=False,
                timeout=timeout_seconds,
            )
```

When the row stores no command the re-run never executes;
`reckon/crew/promotion.py:626-627` records `"no gate command is stored to
re-run"` as the reason. Chain in one line: committed row's
`gate_check.command` -> line 608 -> `sh -c <command>` with the checkout as cwd.

## Every verify-gate command-line flag

Declared at `reckon/cli.py:2954-2975`, and confirmed against the runtime
`--help` of this worktree's own source:

- `--project` (required) — names the project whose committed ledger holds the run.
- `--run` (required; parameter `run_id`) — names the run whose stored gate command and base verdict are read, and whose row receives the report.
- `--checkout-path` (required, path) — the checked-out repository holding the merged integrated revision; the tree the command runs in, and the ledger root (`root=checkout_path`, `reckon/cli.py:2994`).
- `--revision` (default `HEAD`) — the integrated revision the re-run is attributed to; the command runs only when the checkout's HEAD equals it (`reckon/crew/promotion.py:635-641`), otherwise the report records `integrated_verdict: not-run`.
- `--timeout-seconds` (float, default 300.0) — the bound on the re-run; a timeout records `timed_out: true` and `not-run`, never `passed`.
- `--pretty` — indents the JSON output; presentation only.

## Verdict

`missing-option`. The surface already executes a stored command at the merged
head under a timeout, records the report on the run's row and commits it, and
the command is read from exactly one place. Nothing in that path depends on
the command's identity.

- Not `existing-option`: the six flags above are exhaustive; no flag, environment value or other stored field changes the command.
- Not `missing-surface`: a new command would re-implement the tree check, the timeout, the report and the commit that verify-gate already owns.
- The whole-repository suite command is already a first-class recorded value: flight config `gates.suite_command` (`reckon/schema/flight.yaml:554-558`), resolved at dispatch (`reckon/crew/dispatch.py:3366`), stored on each armed run's pointer (`reckon/crew/dispatch.py:3481`) and carried onto the committed row at promotion (`reckon/crew/promotion.py:2211`, `:2254`, `:3482`; the row keeps it at `reckon/ledger.py:1315`).

Positive control for the absence claim: the same source scan finds `--revision`
(`reckon/cli.py:2963-2967`) and `--timeout-seconds` (`reckon/cli.py:2968-2974`),
and the runtime help of this checkout's own Python lists exactly those six
options.

## What would have to change (one sentence)

For a whole-repository suite to run at the merged head, verify-gate must be
able to be told which command to run — a new option on the subcommand, such as
`--suite` reading the recorded `gates.suite_command`, or a free-form
`--command TEXT`, whose value replaces the read at line 608 — and the file that
would change is `reckon/crew/promotion.py`, with the flag declared in
`reckon/cli.py`.

## Adjacent facts a ship decision needs

- The hand-check had two halves: run the suite at the merged head (the half
  resolved above: one option), and compare failing rails against a baseline
  revision — the second has no home in this path, because `_merged_gate_finding`
  compares the run's base verdict against the integrated verdict, one tree, one
  command.
- The ledger already defines the baseline/after suite observation shape and the
  new-failure set difference (`reckon/ledger.py:235-310`), reusable by a
  merged-head re-run.
- `suite_delta` is null for unarmed projects (`reckon/crew/promotion.py:2254-2256`),
  so the proposed option is meaningful only where the project declares
  `gates.suite_command` in its flight config.

§7 — What this plan does not own

crew-worker · 2026-09-17

The shipped unreconciled-run grace is now fifteen minutes, clearing the two-to-six minute promotion beat the five-minute value sat inside. The declared default lives in the shipped resolution layer, reckon/schema/flight-defaults.yaml, at fences.unreconciled_run_grace; it was 5m and is now 15m. With three fleets reporting the same refill refusal mid-verification, the old value refused a coordinator for doing the merge, marker check at the merged head and promotion that the fence exists to force.

Reproduced at base 7dfae503 and cleared by the change. A focused test resolves the shipped layer with the host and project layers absent and converts the value with the parser dispatch compares a pointer's age against: at base the resolved grace was 300 seconds against the required 900, giving 2 failed, 80 passed, 2 skipped. Landed, the prescribed gate (tests/test_unreconciled_grace_default.py tests/test_flight.py) is 82 passed, 2 skipped, zero added, and a wider cohort over the fence's assertions is 92 passed, 2 skipped. Controls keep the grace a tunable: a project layer at 3m and a caller override at 90s each still win.

The generated artifacts are unchanged, and the agreement check was shown to fire. The generators read reckon/schema/flight.yaml alone, so a default change regenerates nothing and git status confirms neither reckon/_flight_schema.py nor docs/_shared/flight.schema.json moved. The round-trip tests are skipped on this host for want of the generator toolchain, so the new test compares the source slot against the committed pydantic pattern and JSON Schema pattern itself — mutating the source pattern and leaving the artifacts alone turned it red, and reverting restored a clean diff.

Fence note for the next dispatch: two of the files this change required were outside the declared scope. The default is declared in reckon/schema/flight-defaults.yaml, not the reckon/schema/flight.yaml the brief named, and tests/test_flight.py asserts the shipped value by literal so the node's own gate could not be green without it. Two further declared paths, reckon/crew/_flight_schema.py and reckon/flight.schema.json, do not exist. Full record: unreconciled grace evidence.

All three remainders are one claim. A single run, terminal for eight hours with its process gone, held the file that §3's repair, §6's repair and §9's last case all need — and the same file that carried tonight's data-loss defect. Nothing in the surface releases a claim whose run is finished and whose process is dead, so the claim outlived every purpose it had.

Four items were added to this plan by measurement during execution, not by planning: the wiring node, because a derivation landed with no caller; the write-scope agreement, because the check §9 installed enforced a stricter contract than promotion; the discriminating fixture, because a reviewer proved a case could not fail for the right reason; and the caller's scope, because a resolution landed correct and unreachable. Every one came from a review or a worker rather than from the coordinator reading the plan.

What this session cost the coordinator by hand, in this plan's own terms: four gate logs written because nothing produced them, four workers resumed because nothing resumes a process that simply stopped, and one mis-cited gate log that the evidence guard accepted because it reads a marker rather than the log. Each is recorded in its section above.

The independent review measured the consequence — crew check-manifest passes neither value, so the resolution falls back to the caller's working directory and the two surfaces still disagree whenever the caller is not standing in the run's worktree. The mechanism is right and unreachable through the command.

That is the third time in one session on this plan. audit_manifest existed with no production caller; run_rows landed green with no production caller; and now a resolution lands correct while its only command-line caller supplies neither argument it needs. §8's rule was written after the first two and has now been confirmed by a third case it did not predict, which is the strongest form of support a rule of that kind gets. The remaining node is the caller, not the mechanism.

The worker could not have satisfied the claim, which is §11: the declaration is never supplied to the worker, so a verbatim match is unreachable by construction and every such claim in this plan's records is suspect for the same reason. The correction is appended rather than edited into the record above, because a comment is structured state and a claim that was made should stay visible beside the correction to it.

This is the second false verbatim claim a review has caught on this plan, the first being on the gate-log preservation node. Two occurrences, one cause, now recorded as a section rather than repaired twice.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T16:55:00+00:00

§9 closes, and the shape of how it closed is the part worth keeping. The section began as one defect — a manifest contract checked after the worker is gone — and the repair for it exposed three more, each found by an independent review of the node that preceded it, each narrower than the one above: exact membership against containment, then the absolute declaration, then the caller that supplied no scope, then the reclaimed worktree resolving as its own repository. Four layers, four nodes, and every one of the last three was invisible to the gate of the node that caused it.

Verified at the merged head, not from a manifest: 26 cases green in the agreement file, the control reddening exactly the six reclaimed cases, and the four-file cohort carrying the same single pre-existing failure id at base and at head.

What the thread demonstrates about measures. Each node's own gate passed at every stage; nothing in the chain was caught by the party that wrote it. A node's gate measures the diff it just made, and a contract that two surfaces must share cannot be measured from one of them — which is the argument §8 makes about naming the entry point a caller reaches, arrived at from the opposite direction.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T16:11:08.396Z

promotion no longer resolves a reclaimed run's worktree as the repository, so both scope surfaces resolve through the run record's worktree and agree for a run whose worktree is gone; 26 passed at the merged head, the control reddens exactly the six reclaimed cases, and the four-file cohort carries the same single pre-existing failure id at base and head. Closes the four-layer thread of section 9

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T17:45:00+00:00

A manifest that records nothing satisfies the write-time check vacuously. Found by accident, not by looking: a node delivered status: complete with commits, changed_paths and tests all empty, over two real commits sitting in its worktree. The worker ran crew check-manifest against it and reported ok: true, no findings — correctly, by the contract as written.

The reason is the shape of the guard. _require_commit_for_changed_manifest refuses a complete manifest that names an in-repo path with no commits. A manifest naming no paths at all gives it nothing to object to, so the check passes hardest on the manifest that records least. It is the same defect the reviews kept finding in test assertions this session — a check that cannot fail for the case it exists to catch — sitting in the checking machinery rather than in a test.

What it costs. The hollow manifest was recoverable only because the work was committed and the coordinator read the worktree rather than the manifest. Had the check been trusted, a node with two commits would have promoted recording neither, and the row would have cited nothing while every check on it passed. That is §3's defect reached through a different door.

The repair is a floor, not a new guard: a manifest declaring complete asserts that something was delivered, so it should name at least one of a commit, a changed path, or an explicit statement that it produced none and why — the same shape as the --no-commit reason promotion already requires.

where-a-manifest-is-written-and-what-could-check-it — delivered report

Cited from /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reports/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/manifest-write-time-check.md.

# A manifest write-time check: where the manifest is written, what checks it at promotion, and whether a validator already exists

Investigation for plan §9 of `reckon:work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator` (plan version 38),
against worktree revision `31c976a4bb08651d86db3df119d661473c2b111c`.

The manifest is written while the worker still has turns; every check on it today fires at
`crew complete`, after the process that wrote it has ended. This report maps the writers, the
promotion-side readers, and the one reusable entry point that already exists.

## 1. Every code path that writes or templates a worker manifest

1. `reckon/crew/prompts.py:compose_prompt` (definition at `:113`) — **the only template a worker
   receives**. The `MANIFEST (write exactly these keys …)` block is `:249`–`:271`; it is an inline
   f-string in the return value, not a separate constant. Per-node substitutions inside the block:
   `orientation_write_paths` is `json.dumps(list(node.write_paths))` at
   `:198` and rendered at `:252`; `node: {node.id}` at `reckon/crew/prompts.py:253`; the delivery
   path at `reckon/crew/prompts.py:247`. The body template the worker fills is therefore not a data
   structure anywhere — it is prose in this function.
2. `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:dispatch` (`:2422`) — chooses the manifest path and, with it, the
   worker's delivery contract: `durable_manifest = str(run_dir(resolved_run_id) / "manifest.md")`
   (`reckon/crew/dispatch.py:2422`), defaulted at `:2424` and refused if the caller named a tmpfs
   path (`reckon/crew/dispatch.py:2426`–`:2429`). It writes no content.
3. `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:_needs_help_for_question` (`:1563`) — **overwrites the worker's manifest**
   when the peer channel expires: the NEEDS-HELP body is assembled at `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:1569`
   –`:1584`, written to the channel report at `:1587`, and then to the run's manifest at
   `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:1592` (`manifest.write_text(report, encoding="utf-8")`). This is a second
   templating site: the body repeats manifest keys (`status`, `commits`, `changed_paths`, `tests`,
   `test_logs`, `artifacts`-family) with no schema consulted.
4. `reckon/crew/recovery.py:_derive_missing_manifest` (`:2843`) — fabricates a manifest for a terminal
   run that delivered none, text from `reckon/crew/recovery.py:_derived_manifest_text` (`:2817`,
   key block at `:2825`–`:2840`), written with an exclusive create at
   `reckon/crew/recovery.py:2869`–`:2870` (`manifest.open("x")`). Guarded: only when the run has no
   commits beyond base (`:2858`) and has diff paths or a final message (`:2865`).
5. Shape source both the template and the promotion reader must agree with:
   `reckon/crew/reports.py:_MANIFEST_LIST_KEYS` (`:60`) and `reckon/crew/reports.py:_MANIFEST_SCHEMA`
   (`:120`). Not a writer, but the only place a field's accepted shapes are declared, and the natural
   home for the check's schema.

Observed but with no in-repo writer: `~/.config/reckon/crew/manifests/` holds one file per node id
(e.g. `a-block-scalar-ends-where-the-fields-resume.md`). A grep for `manifests` path forms under
`reckon/` returns nothing; the same instrument on `manifest.md` returns `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:2422`,
so the live store is the run directory (`runs/<run-id>/manifest.md`) and this directory is a legacy
or externally-written store. Do not build the check against it.

## 2. Every check the promotion contract applies to a manifest field

In the order the guards run, `reckon/crew/promotion.py:complete` (`:2088`) then
`reckon/crew/promotion.py:_complete_locked` (`:3138`):

| field(s) read | check | field-level helper |
|---|---|---|
| `status`, `changed_paths`, `commits` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_commit_for_changed_manifest` (`:834`, called at `:2148`) — refuses a `status: complete` manifest naming an in-repo path with no `commits` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_prose_changed_paths_name_no_paths` (`:766`), `_changed_paths_inside_repository` (`:785`) |
| `status` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_recognised_manifest_status` (`:2005`, called at `:2149`) — a status word outside the reader's vocabulary is carried forward as a `ManifestParseError` naming the run | `reckon/crew/reports.py:parse_manifest` (`:272`), `reports.py:ManifestParseError` (`:249`) |
| `commits` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_gate_evidence` (`:236`, called at `:2154`) — declared-list read at `reckon/crew/promotion.py:300`-`:302`; a passing gate presenting a strict subset of the manifest's declared commits is *reported* (`:324`-`:340`), never refused | — |
| `changed_paths` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_review_waiver` (`:2037`, called at `:2167`) via `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_manifest_repository_paths` (`:1989`) — a manifest naming an in-repo path makes an independent review mandatory | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_changed_paths_inside_repository` (`:785`) |
| `baseline_suite`, `after_suite`, `failure_attribution` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_evaluate_suite_delta` (`:2247`, called at `:2211`) — reads at `reckon/crew/promotion.py:2266`-`:2267` and `:2309`-`:2311`; writes the refusal onto the pointer (`:2293`, `:2329`, `:2349`) | `reckon/ledger.py:suite_observation_missing_fields` (`:235`), `ledger.normalized_suite_observation` (`:276`), `ledger.added_failure_ids` (`:292`), `ledger.new_failure_attribution` (`:342`) |
| `negative_control_log` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_declared_negative_control` (`:3047`, called at `:3417`) — the field this plan's §9 refusal came from | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_negative_control_log_text` (`:3026`) |
| `commits` | `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_declared_manifest_commits` (`:1431`), read at `:1437` to populate the ledger row (`:3219`, `:3574`) — a read for recording, not a refusal | — |

The `negative_control_log` read verbatim — one line, promotion.py:3104-3106:

```python
    delivered = (
        "" if manifest is None else str(manifest.get("negative_control_log") or "")
    )
```

and the refusal it feeds — promotion.py:3110-3116 verbatim:

```python
3110	            f"run {run_id!r} writes a check ({', '.join(test_paths)}) and declares "
3111	            f"the mutation {declaration!r} in its {NEGATIVE_CONTROL_FIELD} field, "
3112	            "but its manifest carries no negative_control_log path. Promotion "
3113	            "refuses a passing gate whose negative control was never run: keep the "
3114	            "log that mutation produced beside the passing one and name its path "
3115	            "in the manifest as `negative_control_log: <path>`"
3116	        )
```

```python
3126	    if declaration not in text:
3127	        raise CrewError(
3128	            f"run {run_id!r} declares the mutation {declaration!r} but the log at "
```

### Reproduction of the recorded defect (this node's own run)

The plan records two refusals on 2026-09-21 in imas-efit: a mapping-shaped
`negative_control_log` and a markdown-heading-shaped one. Reproduced against this revision by
calling `reckon/crew/reports.py:parse_manifest` and `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_declared_negative_control`
directly on both shapes; log at
`/home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T124048265335-where-a-manifest-is-written-and-what-could-check-it/repro_negative_control.log`:

- mapping shape (`declared`/`log`/`result` sub-keys) → parse keeps a **dict**, the guard's `str()`
  turns it into a path, and the refusal is
  *"names negative_control_log "{'declared': …, 'log': …, 'result': …}", which cannot be read, so the
  mutation it was to evidence was never shown to fail"*;
- heading shape (a markdown heading with the path beneath) → the field is **absent from the parse**
  and the refusal is *"carries no negative_control_log path"*.

Both refusals are correct by the contract and unreachable by the writer, which is the gap §9 names.
Note the field is **not in the reader's schema at all**: `_MANIFEST_SCHEMA` (`reckon/crew/reports.py:120`)
omits `negative_control_log`, so `parse_manifest` never coerces it and the guard is the only code that
gives it meaning.

Measured key census (`schema_diff.log` beside this file): the dispatch template carries **22** keys,
`_MANIFEST_SCHEMA` declares **18**. The six template keys with no schema entry are
`checkpoint`, `negative_control_log`, `resume_brief`, `wait_condition`, `wait_probe`, `wait_terminal`;
the schema holds two keys the template does not carry (`derived`, `needs_help`), both written by
recovery rather than by a worker.

## 3. Does a reusable validation entry point already exist?

**Yes: `reckon/crew/reports.py:audit_manifest` (`:941`)** —
`audit_manifest(text: str, node: TaskNode | None = None, *, suite_armed: bool = False) -> {"manifest", "findings", "ok"}`.
It is the nearest and best candidate, and it already closes the width the fix needs:

- it takes manifest **text**, not a run record, so a worker can call it while it still has turns;
- it takes the `TaskNode` (`reckon/crew/prompts.py` composes the prompt from the same object), so the
  changed-path/write-fence check at `reckon/crew/reports.py:1013`-`:1021` is reachable at write time;
- it converts a parse refusal into a **finding rather than an exception** (`:950`-`:958`), which is the
  right shape for a refusal a worker is meant to act on;
- it already checks the armed suite pair field-by-field (`:967`-`:998`) with the same
  `reckon/ledger.py` helpers promotion uses, so the check can be shared rather than duplicated.

What it does **not** cover, measured:

- `negative_control_log` — the field whose shape caused both recorded refusals. It is absent from
  `_MANIFEST_SCHEMA`, so `audit_manifest` never looks at it, and the only implementation of its
  contract is `reckon/crew/promotion.py:_require_declared_negative_control` (`:3047`). A write-time
  check built from "what exists" must either extend the schema and the audit, or promotion must
  delegate to a shared helper.
- It has **no production caller** under `reckon/` — the review, the CLI and the MCP surface all
  reach the manifest through `parse_manifest` or read it as a file. Its only callers are
  `tests/test_crew.py`, `tests/test_gate_evidence.py`, `tests/test_manifest_parse_format.py` and
  `tests/test_crew_recovery.py`; it is re-exported on the crew facade through the export grid at
  `reckon/crew.py:193`. A write-time check that reuses it **creates its first production
  caller**, which is the wiring gap this node flags for the build node.

Nearest rejected candidate: `reckon/crew/reports.py:parse_manifest` (`:272`). Rejected because it
answers "can this text be read?" and not "is this delivery complete?": a manifest that is silent on
`tests` and names no commit is accepted by it, and for the promotion-relevant fields
(`baseline_suite`, `after_suite`, `negative_control_log`, `changed_paths`) a missing field's silence
is indistinguishable from a legal absence at that layer — and the promotion-relevant absence rule
(`tests` in `test_paths` means `negative_control_log` is required) depends only on the node, which
`parse_manifest` does not receive and `audit_manifest` does.

A third rejected candidate, `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:_apply_orientation_check` (`:4380`), is the only
other code that judges a manifest while a worker could still be running (it runs on observe/attach,
`:4393`); rejected on a narrower test: it validates the three `orientation_*` keys against the pointer
and hard-sets `phase: blocked` on a mismatch rather than returning findings, so it is neither general
nor addressed to the worker whose file it judges.

## Repository paths a write-time check would have to modify

reckon/crew/reports.py
reckon/crew/promotion.py
reckon/crew/prompts.py
reckon/cli.py
reckon/mcp.py
reckon/crew.py
reckon/ledger.py
tests/test_gate_evidence.py
tests/test_manifest_parse_format.py
tests/test_crew.py

Conditional in that list: `reckon/crew/promotion.py` is required only because it holds the sole
implementation of the `negative_control_log` contract (`:3047`) — the check must not become a second
implementation of one field's contract, which is the drift the SPA-binding and §9's own argument
name. `reckon/ledger.py` is required only if the suite-field validation is extended; the existing
helpers are already there. The test paths are where every existing `audit_manifest` / `parse_manifest`
assertion lives, and they are the three files that would move when the schema changes.

## Evidence inputs for writeback

- Revision: `31c976a4bb08651d86db3df119d661473c2b111c` (worktree HEAD of this node).
- Defect reproduced: mapping shape → "cannot be read"; heading shape → "carries no … path"; log
  `repro_negative_control.log` in the run directory.
- Key census: 22 template keys vs 18 schema keys; `negative_control_log` absent from the schema.
- `audit_manifest` (`reckon/crew/reports.py:941`) is the nearest reusable entry point and has no
  production caller today.

§10 — A fence that can only be drawn once

reckon-ship · 2026-09-22T13:35:00+00:00

The command lands, and it carries two controls rather than one. A blocked run's write scope is widened through its own live pointer, so the run keeps its session and its commits. Six cases green, re-run at the merged head rather than taken from the manifest. The declared mutation — declared.append(path) becoming pass, so the command reports a grant it never persists — reddens exactly test_widening_a_blocked_run_adds_the_named_path, which is the assertion that would otherwise pass vacuously. The worker's own first control, removing the blocked-phase guard, reddens test_widening_a_working_run_is_refused_and_writes_nothing and is a finding in its own right: without it a run already writing against its boundary has that boundary moved while its operator reads success. Source restored byte-identically after each, empty diff both times.

This section proved its own cost while being built. §3's node needed one file added to its fence — tests/test_crew_gate_log_agrees.py, whose assertions encode the behaviour §3 supersedes. No command could add it. The choices were to redispatch and discard nearly two hours of that worker's accumulated context, or to hand-edit its live pointer, which are exactly the two moves this section exists to remove. I routed around it with a separate node, which worked only because that particular repair happened to be separable. Measured rather than argued, and it happened to a coordinator who had just written the section.

What remains here: promotion honouring a widened scope end to end. The pointer is what promotion already reads, so the mechanism should hold, but it is unasserted and therefore not claimed.

A note on the impl figure, which now understates the remaining work. The executable node set has grown from thirteen to seventeen during execution, every addition from measurement rather than planning. The raw fraction is thirteen of seventeen, about 0.76; impl is held monotonic by contract and so reads 0.88. Trust the open list, not the gauge: §3's copy and its retarget, §6's option, §9's reclaimed worktree, and this section's end-to-end assertion.

    <h2>Roster registration commits its own write</h2>
    <p><strong>Defect reproduced before repair.</strong> The public member-add command exited successfully while <code>git status --porcelain</code> reported <code>?? docs/</code>; source inspection found no commit anywhere in the registration path. The failing falsifier landed in commit <code>588c56c4</code>.</p>
    <p>Commit <code>0b73f64e</code> added an explicit, default-off commit option to <code>ledger.register_member</code> and enabled it only from <code>crew_member_add</code>. The command now stages and commits only <code>docs/state/&lt;project&gt;/crew.json</code> under a <code>chore(roster):</code> subject with a non-empty body and no parsed trailers. Internal and direct registrations retain the prior non-committing default, so dispatch authority and timing are unchanged.</p>

  <section id="dispatch-derivation-read">
    <h2>§5 — Dispatch reads derivations without composing project state</h2>
    <p>Commit <code>5ff24c49</code> moved the repository derivation read onto the authored
    project manifest. Both legacy aggregate storage and distributed project resources now supply
    the same normalised mapping without sprint hydration or plan-document parsing.</p>

    <h3>The recorded defect still reproduced</h3>
    <p>A valid <code>crew dispatch --dry-run</code> at base
    <code>83a0fb7e0f933b2d8146f1ec4c6d5b1313da29b6</code>, profiled with cProfile on
    <code>all_debug</code>, spent 30.475 of 31.863 profiled seconds in
    <code>_repository_scope_claims</code>. Its 41 scope-entry resolutions made 29 calls to
    <code>_project_derivations</code>; each entered <code>compose_project_state</code> and
    <code>_hydrate_items</code>, producing 285 calls to <code>_plan_html.read_state</code>.</p>

    <table>
      <thead>
        <tr><th>Profile marker</th><th>Base</th><th>After</th><th>Mechanism result</th></tr>
      </thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Live scope-entry resolutions</td><td>41</td><td>42</td><td>Input population remained comparable.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Derivation reads</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>The live pointer population differed by one.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Full project compositions</td><td>29</td><td>0</td><td>Removed from derivation lookup.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Sprint-item hydrations</td><td>29</td><td>0</td><td>Removed from derivation lookup.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Plan HTML state parses</td><td>285</td><td>0</td><td>Independent of pointer count.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>All profiled function calls</td><td>47,736,679</td><td>2,256,308</td><td>The eliminated work is visible beyond wall time.</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p>The after profile ran on the login host after the mechanism had become a short operation;
    its 28 direct derivation reads totalled 0.209 profiled seconds. The profiles therefore prove
    the call-chain removal by call count rather than using cross-node wall time as the gate.</p>

    <h3>Falsifier and positive controls</h3>
    <p><code>tests/test_dispatch_reads_derivations_cheaply.py</code> synthesises three mounted
    projects with three plans each, then resolves claims with 1, 3 and 7 live pointers. The
    observed plan-state parse counts are <code>[0, 0, 0]</code>. The same file proves that the
    direct mapping is byte-identical to <code>compose_project_state</code> for both legacy and
    distributed storage, that an absent mapping returns <code>{}</code>, and that the fixture is
    isolated from the real crew home and mounted docs roots.</p>

    <h3>Gate delta and receipts</h3>
    <p>The prescribed cohort at the base reported 27 failures and 265 passes. At
    <code>5ff24c49</code> it reported the same 27 failure identifiers and 270 passes: zero added
    failures, with all five new tests passing. The pre-existing failures are retained in the
    receipts rather than converted into an absolute-green claim.</p>

  <section id="s5-wall-time-and-width-at-start">
    <h2>§5 — Per-node wall time and width-at-start</h2>
    <p><code>reckon/crew/summary.py:run_rows</code> returns the project's committed run rows,
    each carrying two derived figures: <code>wall_seconds</code> from that row's own
    <code>dispatched_at</code> and <code>completed_at</code>, and <code>width_at_start</code>
    from the measured spans of the same row set at the row's dispatch instant. The stored
    <code>wall_seconds</code> field is never consulted. The derivation reuses
    <code>reckon/ledger.py:_worker_seconds</code> rather than restating the subtraction, so the
    read and the ledger agree on what a span is by construction. Landed in
    <code>20496d11</code>.</p>

    <h3>What reaches a reader, and what does not</h3>
    <p>A figure the row's stamps cannot support is <code>None</code>, never <code>0</code> — an
    absent measurement and a zero-second span are different facts and the second is a
    plausible-looking lie. A ratio of 0.99 between the widths at two dispatch stamps that are
    close together is possible, so no rounding or floor was applied; the counts are integers
    over the measured spans. <strong>No surface reaches this read.</strong>
    <code>crew ledger --view summary</code> resolves to <code>ledger.summary</code>
    (<code>reckon/cli.py:3122</code>), the aggregate, which carries no per-run rows;
    <code>crew ledger --view records</code> reaches <code>ledger.runs</code>
    (<code>reckon/cli.py:3117</code>), the rows <em>before</em> either figure is derived. The
    seam is therefore one call in an existing view rather than a new view, and wiring it
    touches <code>reckon/cli.py</code>, <code>reckon/ledger.py</code> and
    <code>reckon/mcp_views.py</code>, none of which was in this node's write scope.</p>

    <h3>The recorded defect still reproduced</h3>
    <p>The constraint this section records is that the stored <code>wall_seconds</code> is a
    different quantity from the stamp span. Measured on this repository's own committed ledger
    at <code>20496d11</code> (1182 rows, every one carrying a stored value), against
    <code>ledger._worker_seconds</code> resolved from the worktree:</p>
    <table>
      <thead>
        <tr><th>Stored against stamp-derived</th><th>Rows</th><th>Reading</th></tr>
      </thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Equal</td><td>507</td><td>The stored value happens to be the span.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Within one second</td><td>83</td><td>Rounding only; carries fractional seconds.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>One to ten seconds</td><td>237</td><td>Not rounding.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>More than ten seconds</td><td>355</td><td>Includes two rows off by three orders of magnitude.</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p>The two extremes are
    <code>r-20260812T153712167451-gate-element</code> and
    <code>r-20260812T153726158261-sprint-focus-derived</code>: both dispatch at 15:37 and
    complete after roughly 467 and 457 seconds, and both store <code>1093235.797</code> and
    <code>1093123.291</code>. A reader handed the stored field therefore receives, 592 times in
    1182, a number that is not the span — and twice receives a number roughly 2300 times too
    large. This is why the read derives rather than passes through.</p>

    <h3>Falsifier and positive controls</h3>
    <p><code>tests/test_summary_wall_and_width.py</code> enters through
    <code>summary.run_rows</code> rather than through the arithmetic beneath it, because the
    defect is in the read's answer and a direct call to a helper would pass while the read
    stayed wrong. The fixture ranges the input domain rather than one shape: two rows dispatched
    at the same instant, a row whose stored value disagrees with its own stamps, a row missing
    its completion stamp, and a row missing its dispatch stamp. One test asserts that the real <code>docs</code>
    directory is <em>not</em> the fixture's, so the write-isolation half is checked rather than
    assumed.</p>
    <p><strong>Declared negative control:</strong> <code>make the summary read return the stored
    wall_seconds field instead of deriving the figure from the row own stamps</code>. Applied,
    it reddens three of the five tests — the two that assert a derived figure and the one that
    asserts absence — with <code>assert 4242 == 120</code> and <code>assert 7 is None</code>, and
    leaves the width and isolation tests passing, which is the discrimination the control
    should show. The mutation is verbatim on the first line of the red log, and the green run
    that follows is the same tree with only that line restored.</p>
    <p><strong>Base absence, with a positive control:</strong> <code>def run_rows</code> does
    not occur in <code>reckon/crew/summary.py</code> at base
    <code>63fd372f0d08a9a5e457b8e291f5e94c572da97e</code> (count <code>0</code>) and occurs once
    in the delivered tree, so the empty result is the symbol's absence and not an unaimed
    search.</p>

    <h3>Gate delta and receipts</h3>
    <p>The gate is this node's own cohort; the merged head belongs to a separately dispatched
    test node, and no suite-wide claim is made here. At base the cohort does not exist (the
    file is this node's deliverable); at the delivered tree it is <code>5 passed in 1.20s</code>,
    and under the mutation <code>3 failed, 2 passed in 2.42s</code>. Lint delta against base:
    <code>reckon/crew/summary.py</code> carries one finding at base and one after (the same
    pre-existing <code>UP035</code>), and the delivered test file carries none.</p>
    <ul>
      <li><code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T142336254234-the-summary-read-emits-wall-time-and-width/tests-green.log</code></li>
      <li><code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T142336254234-the-summary-read-emits-wall-time-and-width/negative-control.log</code></li>
      <li><code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T142336254234-the-summary-read-emits-wall-time-and-width/stored-vs-derived.log</code></li>
      <li><code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T142336254234-the-summary-read-emits-wall-time-and-width/manifest.md</code></li>
    </ul>
  </section>

  <section id="s2-roster-commit-content-agreement">
    <h2>Roster commit content agrees with its message</h2>
    <p><strong>The previously recorded mismatch reproduced at base
    <code>51c2b9e0</code>.</strong> A default registration of <code>internal-a</code>
    left the roster uncommitted; a subsequent <code>commit=True</code> registration of
    <code>public-b</code> created one commit whose subject was
    <code>chore(roster): register public-b</code> while its content carried both members.
    The checkout was clean afterward, so the earlier registration had been silently
    absorbed rather than left visible.</p>

    <p>Commits <code>cf9afcf1</code> and <code>c57ec115</code> choose the explicit-refusal
    design. Before writing, a committing registration now inspects only the roster path.
    If that path is dirty, it compares the current roster with <code>HEAD</code>, names the
    blocking registrations, and refuses. The reproduced sequence now reports
    <code>cannot commit roster registration 'public-b': uncommitted member registration(s):
    internal-a</code>, creates zero commits, retains <code>internal-a</code>, and never writes
    <code>public-b</code>.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base</th><th>After</th><th>Result</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Dirty-roster sequence</td><td>1 commit; message names <code>public-b</code>; content carries 2 members</td><td>0 commits; refusal names <code>internal-a</code>; content carries 1 member</td><td>Message/content ambiguity is impossible without discarding the earlier write.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Focused controls</td><td>New falsifier failed because no refusal occurred</td><td>3 passed</td><td>Clean public add still commits once; default still writes without committing; unrelated dirty content stays unstaged and uncommitted.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Roster/member cohort on <code>all_debug</code></td><td>33 failed, 338 passed</td><td>33 failed, 339 passed</td><td>0 added and 0 removed failure IDs.</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p>The empty failure delta is instrumented rather than inferred: the known baseline
    failure <code>tests/test_ledger.py::test_completion_promotes_the_pointer_into_the_repositorys_ledger</code>
    appears in both extracted sets. Every repository fixture lives under pytest's temporary
    directory, and the new falsifier asserts the real checkout's status is byte-identical
    before and after the sequence. Full receipts are under
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260917T095804364622-the-roster-commit-carries-only-what-it-names/</code>.</p>
