      </tbody>
    </table>

    <h3>The declared mutation reddened the gate</h3>
    <p>The declared mutation is <em>remove the report-backed source so the composer falls back
    to ledger rows only</em>, applied in a scratch copy of the fixed tree by deleting the
    report pass from <code>_render_document</code> and changing nothing else. Under it the
    focused file reported <strong>2 failed, 2 passed</strong>: the two report-backed
    assertions reddened with the paths and content absent from the section, while the
    ledger-row case and the single-renderer case stayed green — so the red is the declared
    mutation rather than a failure for another reason. The log is
    <code>negative-control.log</code> in this run's directory, with the mutation verbatim on
    its first line.</p>
    <p><strong>A first attempt at that log was invalid and was discarded.</strong> The module
    resolution it printed reported <em>is scratch: False</em>, because <code>python -c</code>
    puts the working directory first on <code>sys.path</code> and the worktree shadowed the
    scratch copy. The re-run resolved from a neutral working directory with the scratch tree
    on <code>PYTHONPATH</code>, printed <em>is scratch: True</em>, and only that run's failure
    count is recorded here.</p>

    <h3>Gate, and what it does not cover</h3>
    <p>The focused gate is <code>tests/test_evidence_from_report.py</code> together with the
    pre-existing <code>tests/test_evidence_synthesis.py</code>: <strong>9 passed</strong> in
    2.81s. The four new cases enter through
    <code>synthesize_landed_record</code> rather than a private helper, and the base-side
    figure for the report-backed behaviour is zero coverage rather than a lower count —
    the report-backed source did not exist, so nothing covered it. What the gate does not
    cover is stated rather than implied: it measures this node's own change, not the merged
    result, and the merged result is verified by a separately dispatched test node. The
    existing five synthesis cases pass unchanged, and the composer's output for a
    commit-bearing run is asserted to stay byte-identical by a case whose report content
    is deliberately marked not-to-be-cited and is asserted absent from the whole
    document.</p>

    <h3>Residue, recorded rather than smoothed over</h3>
    <p>Ranging the manifest reader over the workstation's own committed ledger rows moved a
    large share of previously unresolvable commit-less rows to resolving, and the remainder
    is three honest shapes rather than a parsing defect: manifests deleted with the
    <code>/run/user</code> tree they lived in, run directories that no longer exist, and
    <code>artifacts</code> values written as prose (<code>path — description</code>) rather
    than as a path. Each of those now reaches the reader as a stated absence naming the
    candidate it could not read, which is a truthful section; none of them is cited as a
    report. A flow sequence a worker wrote can also survive the parse as one literal string,
    and that case is decoded rather than treated as a single impossible path.</p>
    <p>Changed product path: <code>reckon/evidence.py</code>. Regression coverage:
    <code>tests/test_evidence_from_report.py</code> (four tests, each building a throwaway
    repository, a temporary crew home and the run directory the manifest cites). Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T142607348054-the-landed-record-composes-from-a-run-report/</code>.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-manifest-check-test-witnesses-its-own-isolation">
    <h2>&sect;9 &mdash; The check-command test witnesses the isolation it depends on</h2>

    <p><strong>The gap was named by the independent review, not by the suite.</strong>
    The review of the node that gave <code>audit_manifest</code> its first production caller
    scored the work 95/100 and spent its one finding on the new test file: the fixture
    redirected <code>RECKON_HOME</code> to a test-config home but never asserted the crew home
    was left untouched, so a broken home resolution would write the fixture's pointer into the
    real directory and every case would still pass. The repository rule names exactly that
    assertion for every write-shaped test, and it names why the write direction is the one that
    matters &mdash; a reader can only be wrong, while a writer can make others wrong. Four
    cases in that file each write a live pointer.</p>

    <p><strong>The obvious watch is the wrong one, and the reason is measured.</strong> A
    byte-for-byte comparison of the production crew home is not merely slow: on a live wave it
    is a moving target. Measured before writing the assertion, that directory held
    <strong>598,286 entries and 138 of them were rewritten inside six seconds</strong> &mdash;
    stream logs growing, pointers appearing and being cleared by concurrent runs, reports
    rewritten mid-wave. A whole-tree comparison there would be slow, would be flaky, and a pass
    over it would be a statement about the fleet rather than about the case under test. So the
    watched root is the crew home this process resolved <em>before</em> a case substitutes one,
    which is the directory a broken resolution would actually write into &mdash; the suite's
    own temporary home, not the production one.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/isolation-witness.svg"
           alt="Left, the watched root: the crew home the process resolved before a case substitutes one, snapshotted entry by entry with its bytes before and after the case and compared. Right, the case's own home, substituted via RECKON_HOME, where the fixture's pointer write is meant to land. A dashed escape arrow runs between them: if home resolution stopped honouring the substitution the pointer would land under the watched root instead, and the comparison would fire naming created: live/&lt;run&gt;.json. Below, the permanent control makes the same write into a substitute root and shows the witness quiet before it and firing after it. A footnote records that the production crew home is not the watched root — 598,286 entries with 138 rewritten in six seconds.">
      <figcaption>The escape the witness exists to catch, and the control that shows it firing
      on a root the production home never sees.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>The order is guaranteed by a dependency, not by luck.</strong> The witness
    fixture requests the suite's temporary-home fixture <em>by name</em>, so the substitution is
    in place before it captures anything and the root it captures cannot be the production
    directory; the manifest fixture in turn depends on the witness, so capture precedes the
    case's write. Ordering by autouse alone would have left the root dependent on fixture
    instantiation order. The witness also refuses to be vacuous: if the root it resolved lies
    under <code>Path.home()</code> it raises, because a pass in that state would be measuring
    the fleet &mdash; the aim assertion, without which a witness pointed at the wrong directory
    reads as silent success, which is the failure shape this plan keeps finding.</p>

    <p><strong>The assertion is shown to fire, on a root the production home never sees.</strong>
    A guard that never fires is indistinguishable from no guard, so the file carries a permanent
    fifth case that constructs the witness over a substitute root under <code>tmp_path</code>,
    calls it once with nothing written (it stays quiet), writes the same pointer the manifest
    fixture writes, and requires the assertion to name the path it caught. The declared mutation
    then proved the same property over the real cases: the fixture was made to write its pointer
    into the watched root as well, unsubstituted, and every command case went red.</p>

    <table>
      <thead>
        <tr><th>Case</th><th>Clean</th><th>Under the declared mutation</th></tr>
      </thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>a manifest inside its fence exits zero</td><td>pass</td><td>fail &mdash; the witness names the watched root</td></tr>
        <tr><td>the command names the run it read</td><td>pass</td><td>fail &mdash; the witness names the watched root</td></tr>
        <tr><td>a path outside the node fence is reported</td><td>pass</td><td>fail &mdash; the witness names the watched root</td></tr>
        <tr><td>an unknown run is refused</td><td>pass</td><td>fail &mdash; the witness names the watched root</td></tr>
        <tr><td>the isolation assertion fires on a substitute root</td><td>pass</td><td>pass &mdash; it writes nothing near the watched root</td></tr>
        <tr><td>the CLI is the only production caller</td><td>pass</td><td>pass &mdash; it reads the source tree, not the home</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p>The red log's first line carries the mutation verbatim, and its tail is
    <code>4 failed, 2 passed in 1.61s</code> / <code>EXIT=1</code>. The assertion named a
    pytest temporary directory as the watched root and the way it moved &mdash;
    <code>the root existed=False before, True after</code> &mdash; because under that mutation
    the root itself was created by the escaping write, which is the <em>existence</em> branch
    of the differ. The <em>path</em> branch is what the control exercises, on a substitute root
    whose <code>live/</code> directory already exists, where the assertion names
    <code>live/r-20260921T000000000000-manifest-check.json</code> exactly. Restoring the file left it byte-identical
    to the committed copy (sha256 <code>ec95cf2d&hellip;</code>), so the mutation is declared and
    reversible rather than a state the evidence was trimmed around. The pattern it was modelled
    on &mdash; and the reason the production home is never a party to it &mdash; is the
    write-outside-the-repository rule: the failing case proves the assertion works without any
    case ever writing into the real directory the assertion protects.</p>

    <h3>The write-time check refused this node's manifest, and it was wrong to</h3>
    <p>The node ran the command it had just given a test to, against its own run. It exits 1 with
    one finding &mdash; <em>changed paths outside the write scope:
    docs/figures/&hellip;/isolation-witness.svg</em> &mdash; and that path is inside the fence: the
    dispatch granted the directory <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator</code>
    verbatim, and the figure was created under it. The two implementations of one contract
    disagree, and the disagreement is measured rather than read off the source. Called side by
    side on this node's own changed paths and declared paths: the write-time test returns the
    figure as stray; the promotion test returns nothing outside.</p>

    <table>
      <thead>
        <tr><th>Implementation</th><th>Where</th><th>Test applied</th><th>Result on the figure</th></tr>
      </thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>write-time audit</td><td><code>reports.audit_manifest</code> at <code>reports.py:1014</code></td><td><code>path not in set(node.write_paths)</code> &mdash; exact membership</td><td>stray, refused</td></tr>
        <tr><td>promotion contract</td><td><code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code></td><td><code>path == root or path.is_relative_to(root)</code> &mdash; root containment</td><td>inside the fence, accepted</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p>The docstring of the containment test calls the declared path a write <em>root</em>, and
    dispatch grants directories, so the stricter reading is the write-time one &mdash; which makes
    the early refusal this section exists to install wrong in a specific direction: it sends a
    worker back to repair a manifest that promotion would accept. That is the same class of harm
    the section names, a refusal aimed at the wrong party, arriving one node later than the late
    one it replaced. It is <strong>reported rather than repaired</strong>: the repair lives in
    <code>reckon/crew/reports.py</code>, outside this node's declared scope. Two candidate repairs,
    for whoever owns that file &mdash; make the write-time test containment-aware so both
    implementations agree, or have dispatch enumerate the files it means rather than granting a
    directory neither side reads the same way.</p>

    <h3>Residue, recorded rather than smoothed over</h3>
    <p>The watched root is the session's shared temporary home, so the same exposure the
    dispatch-derivation test carries applies here: a fixture that arms a watch producer is reaped
    at session end rather than per case, and could in principle write under that root between
    the snapshots. It was not observed in this node's runs, and the exposure is inherited rather
    than introduced &mdash; the sibling file watches the same root by the same mechanism. It is
    recorded here because the alternative was to assert over the production home, which cannot be
    compared whole at all.</p>

    <p>Changed path: <code>tests/test_manifest_check_command.py</code> (six cases; four command
    cases now carry the witness, plus the permanent control). Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T160010767690-the-check-command-test-proves-its-own-isolation/negative-control.txt</code>.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-derived-figures-reach-the-read-surfaces">
    <h2>&sect;5 — The derived figures reach the read surfaces</h2>
    <p>The derivation landed one node earlier and nothing called it. This node is the
    wiring: the two figures now arrive at a reader of the committed-run read.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/derived-figures-reach-the-read-surfaces.svg"
           alt="The committed ledger feeds summary.run_rows, which derives wall_seconds and width_at_start;
                ledger.read_records delegates to it with with_figures on by default, so both the CLI
                records view and the MCP records view receive the figures, while ledger.runs passes
                with_figures off and keeps the stored row for its exact-row callers.">
      <figcaption>One derivation, both read surfaces receiving it. The raw
      <code>ledger.runs</code> path is kept beside it, because a caller asserting the stored row
      byte-for-byte must not receive a derived one.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <h3>The seam, and why it is a default rather than a new view</h3>
    <p><code>reckon/cli.py</code> reaches <code>ledger.runs</code> for <code>crew ledger --view
    records</code>; <code>reckon/mcp.py</code>'s records read calls
    <code>ledger.read_records</code>. Both surfaces were reachable from one function, so the
    derivation sits in <code>read_records</code> behind <code>with_figures: bool = True</code>
    and <code>runs</code> opts out with <code>with_figures=False</code>. Defaulting it on is
    the load-bearing half: the MCP records read is in <code>reckon/mcp.py</code>, and a new
    required parameter there would have been an edit in a file this node does not own, so an
    opt-in flag would have wired one surface and left the other emitting neither figure — the
    exact defect <code>test_the_derivation_has_a_production_caller_inside_reckon</code> exists
    to catch.</p>
    <p><code>runs</code> keeping the stored row is not conservatism. <code>runs</code> is the
    raw promotion-order read and <code>tests/test_ledger.py</code> asserts its rows against the
    stored shape, so derived keys appearing there would have changed a shared shape in service
    of a read that did not need it. The two reads differ in exactly one respect and the
    difference is declared rather than incidental: <code>read_records</code> answers the
    reader's two questions, <code>runs</code> hands back what was committed.</p>
    <p><strong>The stored <code>wall_seconds</code> is not consulted on the derived path.</strong>
    The delegation is to <code>summary.run_rows</code>, which reads the stamps and never the
    field, so a row whose stored value disagrees reports the stamp-derived figure. The
    stored-vs-derived measurement for this repository's ledger is in
    <a href="#s5-wall-time-and-width-at-start">the derivation record above</a> and is not
    restated here.</p>

    <h3>Falsifier and declared negative control</h3>
    <p><code>tests/test_derived_figures_reach_a_reader.py</code> enters through the two surfaces a
    reader actually reaches — <code>CliRunner().invoke(cli.main, ["crew", "ledger", "--project",
    …, "--view", "records", …])</code> and <code>mcp._crew(PROJECT, view="records", …)</code> —
    rather than calling <code>summary.run_rows</code>. A test
    against the deriver would have passed on the base tree, where the deriver is correct and the
    surfaces are empty; this is the reason the file exists separately from the derivation's own
    test.</p>
    <p>The fixture ranges the input domain rather than one shape: two rows dispatched at the same
    instant, a row whose stored value disagrees with its own stamps, a row missing its completion
    stamp, and a row missing its dispatch stamp. The figures asserted are therefore asserted at
    the surface: appearing on every row, being the stamp-derived value where the stored one
    disagrees, and being absent rather than <code>0</code> where the stamps cannot support them.
    Both surfaces are asserted to agree figure by figure, and a filtered read is held to the
    width measured over every committed row — a read counting width after applying its filter
    would report the width of the selection.</p>
    <p><strong>Declared negative control:</strong> <code>read_records: "records =
    summary.run_rows(project, root=root)" -&gt; "records = list(data["runs"])"</code> — the
    derivation removed, so both surfaces pass the stored row through. Applied alone, it reddens
    <strong>all nine</strong> cases, including the static caller test, because the call site it
    searches for is gone with it. <code>assert 4242 == 120</code> and <code>assert 7 is
    None</code> are the two shapes it shows. The mutation is verbatim on the first line of the red
    log, and the green run after it is the same tree with only that mutation reverted.</p>

    <h3>Gate delta</h3>
    <p>The cohort is <code>test_derived_figures_reach_a_reader.py</code>,
    <code>test_summary_wall_and_width.py</code>, <code>test_ledger.py</code>,
    <code>test_mcp_tools.py</code>, <code>test_crew_surface_parity.py</code>,
    <code>test_lane_evidence.py</code> and <code>test_run_store.py</code>, run whole at both
    revisions against a throwaway extraction of the base commit rather than a stash or a second
    checkout.</p>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Revision</th><th>Result</th><th>Failing ids</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>base</td><td>5 failed, 167 passed</td><td>the same five, all in test_ledger.py</td></tr>
        <tr><td>this node</td><td>5 failed, 176 passed</td><td>the same five</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>
    <p><strong>Zero added failures, and the nine new passes are this node's file.</strong> The five
    are pre-existing on both sides and are the shared-environment failures other nodes on this
    machine record; <code>test_ledger.py</code> run alone shows four of them at both revisions,
    so the fifth appears under cross-file ordering rather than under this change. The file is
    green alone: <code>9 passed in 2.17s</code>, against <code>9 failed in 2.82s</code> under the
    mutation. Lint delta: the two changed product files carry no new findings, measured against a
    scratch copy of their base content. This measures this node's own change; the merged head
    belongs to a separately dispatched node.</p>
    <p>Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T155801579049-the-derived-figures-reach-a-reader/</code>
    — <code>cohort-head.log</code>, <code>cohort-base.log</code>,
    <code>cohort-base-ledger.log</code>, <code>cohort-head-ledger.log</code>,
    <code>mutation-red.log</code> (mutation verbatim on line 1), <code>tests-green.log</code>.</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-filter-order-case-separates-the-orders">
    <h2>&sect;5 &mdash; The filter-order case can tell the two orders apart</h2>

    <p><strong>The case existed and could not fail.</strong> The wiring record above holds a
    filtered read to the width measured over every committed row, "a read counting width after
    applying its filter would report the width of the selection". The case asserting that held
    for both orders, because the fixture's selected plan was
    <code>alpha = {early, disagreeing}</code> and those two runs were dispatched at the same
    instant, so each covered the other: the fleet's count and the selection's count were both
    <code>2</code>. A read that filtered first, then counted, passed the case built to exclude it.</p>

    <p><strong>The fixture now separates the orders.</strong> The row sharing
    <code>early</code>'s dispatch instant, <code>disagreeing</code>, holds another plan, and the
    selected <code>alpha</code> carries <code>early</code> and the stamp-less
    <code>no-dispatch</code>. At 10:00 the fleet held two measured runs; the selection holds
    one. The case asserts the fleet's figure at that instant, <code>2</code>, and derives from the
    same fixture that the selection's own count is <code>1</code>, so it reddens whether the read
    reports the selection's figure or a later fixture edit removes the difference it rests on.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/filter-order-case-separates-the-orders.svg"
           alt="A timeline of the five fixture runs: early and disagreeing both span 10:00, but
                disagreeing holds another plan, so the width at early's dispatch instant is 2 for
                the fleet and 1 for the alpha selection; no-completion never measured an end, and
                no-dispatch carries no dispatch stamp so its width is absent."
           width="960" height="400" style="max-width:100%;height:auto">
      <figcaption>Both runs in flight at 10:00, one of them outside the selected plan. That is the
      whole of the discrimination.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>The probe, which is the fixture's own reading.</strong> A driver stages the fixture
    through the case's own repository helper, reads the selected rows at the surface, and counts
    both orders independently from the fixture's stamps. <code>early</code> reports
    <code>pre-filter=2 post-filter=1 surface=2</code>, with the covering rows named
    <code>pre_spans=[early, disagreeing]</code> and <code>post_spans=[early]</code>;
    <code>no-dispatch</code> is <code>None</code> on both because it has no dispatch stamp. The
    probe exits non-zero unless at least one selected row separates the orders, so it is a check
    on the fixture rather than a transcript of it.</p>

    <p><strong>Declared mutation: filter by plan first, then recount the width over the selection.</strong>
    Applied to <code>read_records</code> alone — the rows are narrowed by plan and the width is then
    recomputed from the surviving rows' own stamps, instead of deriving over every committed row and
    selecting afterwards — it reddens exactly the filter-order case:
    <code>1 failed, 8 passed</code>, the assertion reading <code>assert 1 == 2</code> with the
    surface returning the selection's figure. Every other case reads unfiltered, so none of them sees
    the reversal. The mutation is verbatim on the red log's first line, and
    <code>reckon/ledger.py</code> was restored byte-identically (sha256
    <code>4fdfe08d…</code> before and after); the green run is the same tree with the mutation
    reverted, <code>9 passed</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> This measures the case's own discrimination and the
    file's state at this revision. The merged head belongs to a separately dispatched test node, and
    no product file changed here: the only committed path is the test module, whose nine cases are
    the same nine. Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T174334604139-the-filter-order-case-can-tell-the-two-orders-apart/</code>
    — <code>green.log</code>, <code>probe.log</code>, <code>probe.py</code>,
    <code>negative_control.log</code> (mutation verbatim on line 1).</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-two-write-scope-tests-agree">
    <h2>&sect;9 &mdash; The write-time and promotion-time write-scope tests agree</h2>

    <p><strong>The gap was found by a node, reported rather than repaired, and closed here.</strong>
    The node that hardened the write-time manifest check ran that check against its own run and
    was refused for a figure sitting inside the fence the dispatch had granted. Tracing it showed
    two implementations of one contract reading a directory declaration differently:
    <code>reports.audit_manifest</code> tested exact membership, while
    <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code> tested root containment. The repair was
    outside that node's declared scope, so it was reported. This node owns it.</p>

    <p><strong>The repair is one predicate, applied at the write-time audit.</strong>
    <code>path_within_declared_scope</code> (<code>reckon/crew/reports.py</code>) returns true
    when a changed path equals a declared path or lies beneath one, which is the containment
    rule promotion already applied. <code>audit_manifest</code> now calls it in place of the
    membership set test. The promotion-side function is unchanged, so the earlier surface was
    moved onto the later contract rather than the later surface being loosened to match a
    stricter early one.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/scope-agreement.svg"
           alt="A declared write scope holding a directory and a file. One changed path beneath the directory. Before: the write-time audit applies exact membership and reports it stray, red; the promotion test applies root containment and accepts it, green; the two disagree. After: one containment rule, that a target equals a root or the root is one of its parents, applied at both surfaces, and the path is accepted by both. A footnote records that restoring exact membership reddens four of ten cases, the directory cases and a bare directory naming case, while the file-versus-file and out-of-scope cases stay green.">
      <figcaption>Where the two surfaces agree, where they disagreed, and the rule the earlier
      one was moved onto.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>Proven by a test that enters both surfaces for the same inputs, not by asserting
    about either one.</strong> <code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code> calls
    <code>reports.audit_manifest</code> and <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code> with
    one changed path and one declaration, and compares their verdicts. Four properties:
    a changed path equal to a declared path is accepted by both; a changed path under a declared
    directory is accepted by both, which is the case the write-time audit refused before this
    change; a changed path under no declared path is rejected by both; and the two agree on a
    table of seven path-and-declaration pairs covering file declarations, directory declarations
    bare and trailing-slashed, and a prefix case a string-prefix test would wrongly accept
    (<code>reports.py.bak</code> against <code>reports.py</code>, which both reject).</p>

    <p><strong>Falsified before trusting it.</strong> The declared mutation restores the
    exact-membership test so a path under a granted directory is reported stray again. Applied,
    the suite reddens <code>4 failed, 6 passed in 0.82s</code>, <code>EXIT=1</code>: the
    directory case and the three table rows whose declaration is a directory, nine cases total
    passing under the file declaration and the out-of-scope case. The mutation sits verbatim on
    the red log's first line and the source was restored byte-identically, verified by an empty
    <code>git diff</code> against the commit that carries the fix.</p>

    <p><strong>Suite delta measured against the base revision, not against green.</strong> The
    focused cohort — this file, the manifest-check command tests, the promotion-scope tests and
    the manifest-format tests — is <strong>142 passed</strong>. A wider selection over
    <code>tests/test_crew.py</code> matching manifest, audit or scope reported 6 failures, and
    all 6 fail identically with this node's change temporarily removed and the base
    revision's <code>reports.py</code> restored in its place, confirming they are pre-existing
    and unrelated to this change.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/crew/reports.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code>. Commit <code>165463c6</code>. Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T171122683919-the-two-scope-tests-agree-on-a-granted-directory/</code>
    (<code>negative-control.log</code>, <code>after-suite.log</code>).</p>
  </section>

  <section id="a-declaration-is-resolved-before-it-is-compared">
    <h2>&sect;9 &mdash; A declaration is resolved before it is compared</h2>

    <p><strong>Moving a check earlier gave it a stricter contract than the one it was moved
    from.</strong> The write-time manifest audit was installed so a worker learns of a stray path
    while it still holds the pen. It judged a declared write path by exact membership, while
    <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code> judges containment and says so in its own
    docstring. Where dispatch granted a directory the two agreed; where dispatch granted a
    <em>directory written as an absolute path</em>, the audit resolved nothing and the two
    disagreed on every file beneath it. The refusal then reached the worker, which is the
    improvement the earlier node installed, and it was a refusal the worker could not act on
    correctly: obeying it meant editing a manifest promotion would have accepted.</p>

    <p><strong>Reproduced at base <code>71462872</code> before the repair, through both entry
    points with one changed path and one declaration.</strong> An absolute directory declaration
    inside the repository: the write-time audit reports the changed path stray while the
    promotion check accepts it. An absolute file declaration inside the repository: the same
    disagreement. An absolute declaration naming a location outside the repository, and a
    relative directory declaration: both surfaces already agree, the first rejecting and the
    second accepting. The disagreement is therefore specific to an absolute declaration that
    resolves into the repository &mdash; an acceptance suppressed at the earlier surface, not a
    rejection invented at the later one.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/declaration-resolution.svg"
           alt="One dispatch declaring an absolute directory path, with a changed path recorded repository-relative. Before: the write-time audit compares literally and reports the changed path stray, red, while the promotion check resolves the declaration and accepts it, green, so the two disagree. A resolution strip shows the absolute declaration resolving under the repository to a repository-relative root that is then compared. After: the same resolved root is compared at both surfaces and both accept. A footnote records that restoring the literal compare reddens all five absolute inside-repository pairs while the seven relative pairs and the outside-repository rejection control stay green, and that a declaration naming a location outside the repository resolves to no root so both surfaces reject a repository path.">
      <figcaption>Where the resolution belongs, and what a literal compare does without it.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>The repair is the resolution, taken from the surface that already had it.</strong>
    <code>path_within_declared_scope</code> (<code>reckon/crew/reports.py</code>) now maps each
    declared path into a repository-relative root before comparing, delegating to the mapping
    promotion already applies rather than growing a second rule that can drift from it.
    <code>audit_manifest</code> accepts the worktree and repository that mapping needs, defaulting
    to the working directory, which is where the write-time audit runs. A declaration naming a
    location outside the repository resolves to no root, so it rejects a repository path at both
    surfaces rather than at only one.</p>

    <p><strong>Proven by the two surfaces agreeing on inputs built for the disagreement.</strong>
    <code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code> gains a table of five absolute declarations
    naming locations inside the repository &mdash; a directory bare, a directory nested, a
    directory trailing-slashed, a file, and the <code>tests</code> directory &mdash; each paired
    with a changed path beneath it and each asserted equal between
    <code>reports.audit_manifest</code> and <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code>. Two
    named cases assert the directions directly: a changed path under an absolute inside
    declaration is accepted by both, and an absolute declaration outside the repository rejects a
    repository path in both. The seven relative-declaration pairs are unchanged and still pass.
    The file is <strong>17 passed</strong>.</p>

    <p><strong>Falsified before trusting it.</strong> The declared mutation restores the literal
    compare, resolving nothing. Applied, the file reddens
    <code>6 failed, 11 passed</code>: all five absolute inside-repository pairs and the named
    inside case. The eleven that stay green are the seven relative pairs, the three relative named
    cases and the outside-repository rejection control &mdash; a rejection is not flipped by
    removing a resolution step, so the control is correctly unmoved and the table is the part that
    measures the repair. The mutation sits verbatim on the red log's first line, and the source
    was restored to the committed content.</p>

    <p><strong>The six pre-existing failures in the collateral cohort are attributed by
    non-reachability, not by a red base.</strong> The focused cohort &mdash; the manifest, audit
    and scope tests of <code>tests/test_crew.py</code>, plus <code>test_gate_evidence.py</code>,
    <code>test_manifest_parse_format.py</code>, <code>test_manifest_check_command.py</code> and
    this file &mdash; is <strong>6 failed, 165 passed</strong>. All six sit in
    <code>tests/test_crew.py</code> at lines 1373, 2483, 3247 and 3362, which exercise
    <code>plan_dispatch</code> and the worktree and manifest lifecycle. The changed predicate has
    exactly one caller, <code>audit_manifest</code>, whose only production caller is
    <code>crew check-manifest</code>; none of the six tests reaches it, and the six tests that do
    reach it, at lines 4509 to 4553, pass. A failure cannot be caused by a predicate no path in
    the failing test invokes.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/crew/reports.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code>,
    <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/declaration-resolution.svg</code>.
    Commit <code>2c8aa281</code>. Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T180145690656-a-declaration-is-resolved-before-it-is-compared/</code>
    (<code>logs/gate.txt</code>, <code>logs/negative-control.txt</code>,
    <code>logs/cohort-focused.txt</code>).</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-check-command-supplies-the-scope-it-resolves-against">
    <h2>&sect;9 &mdash; The check command supplies the scope it resolves against</h2>

    <p><strong>The resolution step landed on the audit and the command that calls it handed it
    nothing.</strong> The previous node moved <code>path_within_declared_scope</code> onto the
    mapping promotion applies, and gave <code>audit_manifest</code> the worktree and repository
    that mapping needs, defaulting both to the working directory. But <code>crew
    check-manifest</code> kept invoking <code>audit_manifest(text, node)</code> with neither
    argument, so every reading took the default. The two surfaces agreed only when the caller
    happened to stand in the run's worktree &mdash; which a worker does and a coordinator
    checking a delivered run does not. From anywhere else an absolutely declared directory
    resolved under neither tree, and every changed path read stray.</p>

    <p><strong>Reproduced at base <code>bd2a5438</code> before the repair, and quoted.</strong>
    Calling the audit from a directory that is neither the worktree nor the repository, with a
    fence of two absolute directories &mdash; one under the worktree, one under the repository
    &mdash; and a manifest recording both changed paths repository-relative:</p>

    <pre><code>caller cwd: /run/user/39486/tmpf1sms8ec/elsewhere
no worktree/repo (base behaviour): ['changed paths outside the write scope: lib/helper.py, src/module.py']
worktree+repo from pointer:       []</code></pre>

    <p>The same inputs read clean once the tree travels with the call. The base command either
    produced the first line or the second depending only on where it was invoked, which is the
    caller-dependence this node removes. Receipt:
    <code>repro-scope-depends-on-cwd.log</code> in the run directory.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/check-manifest-scope-source.svg"
           alt="One run pointer holding a worktree and a repository, and one absolute directory declaration. Before: the scope is read from the working directory, so a caller in the run worktree resolves the declaration and reports no finding, while a caller elsewhere resolves it under neither tree and reports every changed path stray — the two disagree. After: the scope is read from the run pointer's worktree and repo fields and passed into audit_manifest, so both callers report no finding. A footnote records that dropping the two arguments to fall back to the working directory reddens all three new cases and leaves the six existing ones green, three failed against six passed.">
      <figcaption>Where the scope comes from. A declaration granted absolutely only maps to the
      repository-relative path a manifest records when it is resolved against the tree the run
      worked in.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>The repair reads the two fields the pointer already holds.</strong>
    <code>crew check-manifest</code> (<code>reckon/cli.py</code>) now takes <code>worktree</code>
    and <code>repo</code> from the run's live pointer &mdash; the fields
    <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code> reads from its record &mdash; and passes them
    through to <code>audit_manifest</code>. Nothing in the resolution mapping moved, and no
    product path outside this command changed: the audit was handed arguments it was already
    documented to accept. A pointer recording neither field still falls back to the working
    directory, which is the behaviour a run without a worktree had.</p>

    <p><strong>Proven by three cases that enter through the CLI and stand outside both
    trees.</strong> A fixture writes a run whose worktree and repository are two distinct
    directories, with the fence declared absolutely &mdash; <code>src</code> under the worktree
    and <code>lib</code> under the repository &mdash; so a case can tell which tree a
    declaration was resolved against; a manifest recording <code>src/module.py</code> and
    <code>lib/helper.py</code> repository-relative resolves under both roots only when both
    trees are supplied. Three cases in <code>tests/test_manifest_check_command.py</code>: the
    command invoked from a directory that is neither tree reports no finding; a changed path
    under no declaration, added to the same manifest, is reported and the finding names exactly
    that path and none of the declared ones; and the command invoked from two different
    directories returns the same findings. The file is <strong>9 passed</strong>, the six
    existing cases unchanged.</p>

    <p><strong>Falsified before trusting it.</strong> The declared mutation drops the two
    arguments so the resolution falls back to <code>Path.cwd()</code>. Applied, the file reddens
    <code>3 failed, 6 passed in 1.22s</code>, <code>EXIT=1</code>: all three new cases fail and
    all six existing cases pass, and the outside caller under the mutation reports
    <code>changed paths outside the write scope: lib/helper.py, src/module.py</code> where the
    fixed tree reports none. The undeclared-path case was tightened for this &mdash; its first
    form asserted the stray path merely appeared among the findings, which a run reporting every
    path also satisfies, and it stayed green under the mutation; asserting the finding names
    exactly <code>docs/plan.html</code> makes it redden for the mutation. The mutation sits
    verbatim on the red log's first line and <code>reckon/cli.py</code> was restored to the
    committed content, verified by an empty <code>git diff</code> at that path against
    <code>4e06ae51</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change
    against its own base; the merged head is a separately dispatched test node's measurement.
    No full-suite run is claimed here. The command's other findings &mdash; the completed-status,
    commit, test and armed-suite checks &mdash; are untouched, and the six pre-existing cases
    that cover them pass unchanged.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/cli.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_manifest_check_command.py</code>,
    <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/check-manifest-scope-source.svg</code>.
    Commits <code>4e06ae51</code> and <code>2bb68b24</code>. Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260921T203445733658-the-check-command-supplies-the-scope-it-resolves-against/</code>
    (<code>gate-check-manifest-scope.log</code>, <code>negative-control-check-manifest-scope.log</code>,
    <code>repro-scope-depends-on-cwd.log</code>).</p>
  </section>
  <section id="widening-writes-the-pointer">
    <h2>&sect;10 &mdash; The widening command writes the pointer promotion reads</h2>
    <p><strong>The gap was a fence drawn once.</strong> <code>--write-path</code> occurs exactly once in
    <code>reckon/cli.py</code>, under <code>dispatch</code>, and <code>crew resume</code> accepts
    <code>--advice</code>, <code>--backend</code>, <code>--reason</code> and <code>--print-only</code>
    and nothing that touches scope. A worker that blocks because its scope was too narrow therefore
    forced a choice between a redispatch that discards its session and its commits, and a hand-edit of
    <code>node.write_paths</code> in the live pointer &mdash; the recorded workaround, which is the
    signal that the surface was missing rather than that coordinators were careless. It compounds with
    promotion: <code>--scope-changed</code> records that the scope moved and does not waive the
    validator, so a scope granted in conversation and not in the pointer meets a refusal hours later,
    with the worker long gone.</p>
    <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/widening-writes-the-pointer.svg"
         alt="One blocked run's live pointer before and after the widening: the session id and the recorded commits read unchanged while node.write_paths gains one path. A working run reaches the same command and is refused at exit 1, its pointer bytes identical to before the call." />
    <p>Commit <code>9a6130d1</code> adds <code>crew widen</code> to <code>reckon/cli.py</code>. It reads
    the run's live pointer, refuses unless the phase is <code>blocked</code>, and appends the named path
    to <code>node.write_paths</code> &mdash; the field <code>promotion._outside_declared_scope</code>
    reads &mdash; through the same per-run lock every other pointer writer takes, so a concurrent reader
    never sees a half-written record:</p>
    <pre>crew widen --run &lt;run-id&gt; --write-path reckon/crew/node.py
{"added": ["reckon/crew/node.py"], "node": "widen", "ok": true, "phase": "blocked",
 "run_id": "r-...", "session_id": "s19-codex-20260916",
 "write_paths": ["reckon/crew/runs.py", "reckon/crew/node.py"]}</pre>
    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Result</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td><code>tests/test_fence_widening.py</code>, every case through the CLI runner</td><td>7 passed in 0.6&ndash;1.2s</td></tr>
        <tr><td>The declared control (<code>node.negative_control</code>): report the added path without writing it into the pointer</td><td>1 failed, 6 passed &mdash; exactly the add-and-read-back case. The command exits 0 reporting <code>["reckon/crew/node.py"]</code> while the pointer's <code>node.write_paths</code> still reads <code>["reckon/crew/runs.py"]</code>: the grant exists only in the command output, which is the vacuous pass the read-back exists to catch.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>The additional control: the blocked-phase boundary removed from both checks</td><td>2 failed, 5 passed &mdash; the working-run refusal and the mid-flight flip case. Removing only the outer check reddens nothing (7 passed), because the under-lock re-check catches both.</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Pointer bytes outside <code>write_paths</code> after a widening</td><td>identical: <code>session_id</code> and <code>commits</code> asserted equal, and every other key of both the pointer and its <code>node</code> compared for equality</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Ruff, <code>reckon/cli.py</code></td><td>2 findings at the base, 2 after: the commit adds none (counted as finding lines; ruff's trailing summary line is its fixable tally, not the finding count)</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Ruff and <code>ruff format --check</code>, <code>tests/test_fence_widening.py</code></td><td>0 findings; formatted</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <h3>Two controls: the declared one, and the boundary it holds</h3>
    <p><strong>The declared control is the persistence mutation</strong> &mdash; the declaration the run's
    <code>node.negative_control</code> carries, <em>make the widening command report success without
    writing the new path into the run live pointer, so the grant exists only in the command output</em>.
    Realise it by replacing <code>declared.append(path)</code> with <code>pass</code>: the command still
    reports the path as added and exits 0, while <code>node.write_paths</code> is left as it was. The
    first case reddens (<code>1 failed, 6 passed</code>) on its read-back, which is the assertion that
    would otherwise pass vacuously &mdash; a command that printed a grant it never persisted would look
    identical in its own output. The declared wording is the control log's first line and so is a
    substring of it.</p>

    <p><strong>The additional control removes the blocked-phase boundary</strong>, replacing both
    <code>if phase != WIDENABLE_PHASE:</code> and <code>if locked_phase != WIDENABLE_PHASE:</code> with
    <code>if False:</code>. Under it <code>2 failed, 5 passed</code>: the working run's pointer is
    rewritten in place while its owner reads <code>"ok": true</code>, and the run that flips mid-flight
    is widened. Removing only the outer check is subsumed by the under-lock re-check &mdash; measured,
    that shape passes 7 &mdash; so the boundary control removes both, and both are recorded in the same
    log beside one another. The uncovered behaviour is the boundary the command exists to protect, not
    an API misuse.</p>

    <p><strong>The race the boundary had, and the check that closes it.</strong> An independent review
    found the phase checked once, on a read taken before the per-run lock, while the pointer mutation
    re-read the record under that lock without re-checking it: a run that leaves the blocked phase in
    that window was widened in place. The mutation now re-checks the phase on the record it read under
    the lock and raises before the under-lock write, so a refusal leaves the pointer as the mutation
    found it. A case drives that window by answering the pointer read <code>blocked</code> once and
    <code>working</code> afterwards; it reddens under the boundary control and passes on the fixed
    source. The outer check is kept &mdash; it refuses an obviously wrong request without taking the
    lock &mdash; with the under-lock check as the authoritative one.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change against its
    own base; the merged head is a separately dispatched test node's measurement, and no full-suite run
    is claimed here. Promotion's own reading of the widened <code>write_paths</code> is unchanged and
    untested by this node &mdash; the command writes the field promotion already reads, and the
    end-to-end widening-then-promotion path is a separate measure.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/cli.py</code>, <code>tests/test_fence_widening.py</code>,
    <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/widening-writes-the-pointer.svg</code>.
    The command landed in <code>9a6130d1</code> and the under-lock re-check in
    <code>03aab7e7</code>, but <strong>the labels and figures above hold from
    <code>5c16ea5f</code> onward</strong>, that being the earliest revision that carries them: a reader
    at <code>03aab7e7</code> opens a record that still named the guard removal the declared control and
    stated the boundary control one check wide, and a reader at <code>9a6130d1</code> &mdash; the
    revision the command itself landed in &mdash; finds no record of this node at all, the evidence
    section not landing until <code>40149b03</code>. Receipts in this node's run directory
    <code>r-20260922T122851730217-the-widening-command-writes-the-pointer</code>: the gate log
    <code>widen-green.log</code> (run at <code>5c16ea5f</code>) and <code>widen-red-mutation.log</code>
    (two control sections, both run at <code>03aab7e7</code> &mdash; the declared persistence control
    first, the additional boundary control beneath it).</p>
  </section>


  <section id="the-agreement-cases-cite-the-recorded-copy">
    <h2>&sect;3 &mdash; The agreement cases cite the recorded copy, not the caller's path</h2>

    <p><strong>Four agreement cases were asserting the behaviour the copy step deliberately
    changes.</strong> They read <code>gate_check.log_path == str(cited_log)</code>, which held
    while promotion recorded where the log lay. Once promotion copies the cited log into the run
    directory and records the copy's path, the row names the copy instead.</p>

    <p><strong>The retargeted claim is the pair, not the path alone.</strong> Each of the four now
    asserts that the row's citation <em>lies inside the run directory</em> and that the bytes at it
    <em>equal the cited source's bytes</em>. Either half alone is satisfiable without the evidence
    surviving with it: a path inside the directory proves nothing about its contents, and a
    matching copy proves nothing about where the row points.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/gate-agreement-cites-the-recorded-copy.svg"
           alt="Before: the row's gate_check.log_path is the path the caller cited, which a worker's
                scratch log loses to the reaper. After: promotion copies the cited log into the run
                directory and the row cites that copy, so the four retargeted cases assert both the
                path inside the run directory and the bytes matching the cited source."
           width="960" height="420" style="max-width:100%;height:auto">
      <figcaption>The citation is redirected to a copy; the assertion is the redirect plus the
      identity of the bytes at its far end.</figcaption>
    </figure>

  <section id="the-cited-gate-log-is-copied-where-the-row-can-find-it">
    <h2>&sect;3 &mdash; A promotion copies the gate log it cites where the row can still find it</h2>

    <p><strong>The row cited a path that was true when written and dead when read.</strong>
    <code>crew complete --gate-log-path</code> records where a log <em>lies</em> rather than
    copying it, and a worker's log routinely lies somewhere that will not survive the promotion
    that reads it: <code>/tmp</code> is reaped, and a worktree's gitignored output directory is
    released with the worktree. Every check a reader then runs on the ledger row passes &mdash;
    the path is well-formed, the command is recorded &mdash; and the evidence is gone, which is
    the same defect as a fabricated identifier one layer along. Two coordinators independently
    invented the same compensation, copying 27 gate logs into a reports directory by hand before
    citing them, which is the signal that it belongs in the surface.</p>

    <p><strong>The repair copies the cited log into the run directory and records the copy's
    path.</strong> <code>_preserve_cited_gate_log</code>
    (<code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code>) runs before <code>ledger.build_record</code>, so the
    row names <code>&lt;run_dir&gt;/gate.log</code> &mdash; a directory that outlives the
    worktree and is pruned only by <code>crew gc</code> on a retention window &mdash; instead of
    the transient original. The command line is unchanged; the copy is made by the surface at
    the last moment it still can be, so no coordinator has to remember it.</p>

    <p><strong>What each case pins.</strong> Five, and each is a falsifier under a declared
    mutation rather than a snapshot of the code that happens to hold. A citation outside the run
    directory entirely (the <code>--gate-log-path /tmp/gate.log</code> shape) must be recorded
    <em>inside</em> the run directory and not at the caller's path. The recorded copy must
    resolve <em>after the original is deleted</em>, with the original's bytes, which is what
    reaping and worktree release actually do to it. A citation under the repository's own
    gitignored output directory &mdash; the second place a worker's log really lives, transient
    because the promotion releases the worktree rather than because it leaves the repository
    &mdash; must likewise be copied, by containment and by bytes. A citation <em>already</em>
    inside the run directory must be carried through unchanged: not moved, not duplicated, and
    not copied over a sibling run's log that already sits under the copy's name. And a citation
    this machine cannot read must be left exactly as given.</p>

    <p><strong>The adjudicated carve-out, which is the rule that governs the last case.</strong>
    Preservation is best-effort and changes no verdict. Promotion may run from a machine the
    worker's log never reached, so a cited path that does not resolve here has no text to
    contradict the verdict: it is left exactly as given, nothing is written into the run
    directory in its place, and the gate verdict and exit status are the caller's, which this
    step reads neither of. An earlier brief asked for the opposite &mdash; a refusal for a
    passing gate whose log is absent &mdash; and that request was withdrawn by its author
    because it contradicted a contract the codebase had already reasoned: a log path that does
    not resolve <strong>must not</strong> turn a landed run into a refusal. The pre-existing case
    that states it, <code>test_a_cited_log_path_that_cannot_be_read_is_left_to_the_promotion</code>,
    passes at both the base revision and this node's head, so the contract is honoured rather
    than restated.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/cited-gate-log-preservation.svg"
           alt="Left: the worker's gate log, cited under /tmp where reaping takes it and under the worktree output directory where the promotion that reads it takes it. Middle: promotion resolves the cited path, copies it into the run directory, and hands the row the copy's path; a citation already inside the run directory is carried through with no second copy; the step is best-effort and verdict-neutral, reading no verdict and refusing nothing. Right: the run directory, durable, holding the byte-identical copy, with the ledger row citing it. Below, a lifetime bar showing the /tmp and worktree-output originals ending early against the run directory lasting until crew gc, and a footnote lane recording that a citation which does not resolve on the promoting machine, and a copy that cannot be written, both hand the check on unchanged.">
      <figcaption>Where a cited gate log lives, where its copy lives, and the two cases the step
      leaves exactly as it found them.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>Falsified before trusting it.</strong> The declared mutation removes the copy so
    promotion records the cited gate log path exactly as the caller gave it, leaving the row
    pointing at the original location. Applied, the gate reddens <code>3 failed, 2 passed in
    13.82s</code>: the <code>/tmp</code> citation, the copy-outliving-its-original case and the
    repository-output citation, so the red log is the declared mutation rather than a failure for
    another reason. It leaves green only the two cases where the mutation and the fix coincide by
    construction &mdash; an already-inside citation is a no-op under both, and an unreadable one
    is left as given under both &mdash; so no single no-copy mutation can differentiate those
    two. Each therefore has its own declared control, run and recorded: deleting the early return
    that carries an inside citation through relocates it onto the canned copy name and reddens
    the already-inside case (<code>1 failed, 4 passed</code>), and replacing the best-effort
    carry-through with a raise reddens the unreadable-citation case
    (<code>1 failed, 4 passed</code>). All three mutations were reverted and
    <code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code> is byte-identical to the landed revision, verified by
    an empty <code>git diff</code> at that path.</p>

    <p><strong>Two earlier assertions in this gate were replaced because they proved nothing.</strong>
    Both compared the row's recorded value against the path the test itself had supplied, so they
    held whether or not the copy was made. They now assert the recorded path is inside the run
    directory, that the bytes there are the worker's, and that a sibling's log under the copy's
    name survives &mdash; properties the mutations above break and the path-string comparison
    could not distinguish.</p>

    <p><strong>Delta measured against the base revision, not against green.</strong> The declared
    cohort &mdash; this node's gate, the gate-log agreement tests, the promotion tests and the
    gate-evidence tests &mdash; is measured at the base revision and at the head over the
    identical test set.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base <code>01eeb308</code></th><th>Landed <code>fe1bf820</code></th><th>Delta</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Focused gate (<code>tests/test_gate_log_is_preserved.py</code>, 5 cases)</td><td>3 failed, 2 passed</td><td>5 passed</td><td>the three copy cases recovered</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Declared four-file cohort</td><td>16 failed, 81 passed</td><td>17 failed, 80 passed</td><td>4 added IDs, 3 removed IDs</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Added IDs, by file</td><td>&mdash;</td><td><code>tests/test_crew_gate_log_agrees.py</code> &times; 4</td><td>all outside this node's declared scope</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p>The three removed IDs are this node's own falsifiers failing at base, which is the property
    that makes them falsifiers rather than snapshots. The four added IDs are
    <code>test_promotion_whose_log_and_command_agree_lands_unchanged</code>,
    <code>test_a_recorded_executed_exit_beside_a_quoted_diagnostic_is_accepted</code>,
    <code>test_fixture_command_not_found_beside_a_passing_summary_is_accepted</code> and
    <code>test_an_agreeing_recorded_exit_status_does_not_refuse</code>. Each asserts
    <code>stored["log_path"] == str(&lt;the path the test itself passed&gt;)</code>, which is
    precisely the citation this change rewrites when the cited log lies outside the run
    directory: the row must name the durable copy, so that assertion cannot hold. They are
    reported for redispatch rather than triaged here, because the file is outside this node's
    write fence and editing it would encode the pre-change behaviour as correct.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change against
    its own base; the merged head is a separately dispatched test node's measurement. No
    full-suite run is claimed here.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_gate_log_is_preserved.py</code>,
    <code>docs/plans/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator.html</code>,
    <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/cited-gate-log-preservation.svg</code>.
    Base revision <code>01eeb308</code>; commits <code>bd576e66</code>, <code>54d0a8d2</code>,
    <code>1911969e</code> and <code>fe1bf820</code>, which is the revision these claims hold at.
    Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260922T113256452748-the-cited-gate-log-is-preserved-where-the-row-can-find-it/</code>
    (<code>test-logs/head-cohort.log</code>, <code>test-logs/base-cohort.log</code>,
    <code>negative-control.log</code>, <code>negative-control-relocation.log</code>,
    <code>negative-control-refusal.log</code>).</p>
  </section>

  <section id="a-reclaimed-worktree-resolves-against-its-recorded-path">
    <h2>&sect;9 &mdash; A reclaimed worktree still resolves the declaration it was granted</h2>

    <p><strong>The two write-scope surfaces agreed on every case except the one where the
    worktree is gone.</strong> Promotion reads a run's worktree when it is a directory and falls
    back to the repository when it is not, because the reclaimed tree cannot be read. The
    write-scope test was resolving a declared write path against that same fallback, and the
    fallback is wrong for resolution: an absolute grant made under the vanished worktree maps to
    no repository-relative root once the worktree path is replaced by the repository, so a changed
    path beneath it read as in scope at the write-time manifest check and as stray at promotion
    — the same declaration, the same run, two verdicts.</p>

    <p><strong>Reproduced at base <code>34f7f5f3</code> through both entry points, and
    quoted.</strong> A run whose recorded worktree does not exist, a repository that does, one
    declaration written absolutely under the worktree, and one changed path beneath it recorded
    repository-relative:</p>

    <pre><code>worktree exists: False
check-manifest findings: []
promotion outside:       ['docs/figures/x/scope.svg']
AGREE: False</code></pre>

    <p>The declaration is correct and the run is sound; only the tree lost when the directory
    was removed. Receipt: <code>repro-reclaimed-scope.log</code> in the run directory.</p>

    <p><strong>The repair makes the recorded worktree the authority for resolution whether or
    not it exists on disk</strong>, which is the tree the write-time check already resolves
    against — it reads the pointer's <code>worktree</code>, which never falls back. Both
    promotion-side scope surfaces now resolve through the same recorded path, so a grant under a
    reclaimed worktree accepts the same changed paths at both. Redispatch entry: the same
    reproduction after the change reports <code>promotion outside: []</code> and
    <code>AGREE: True</code> (<code>repro-reclaimed-scope-head.log</code>).</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/reclaimed-worktree-resolution.svg"
           alt="A run pointer holding a declaration written absolutely under a worktree that no longer exists, and a repository that does. Before: the write-time check resolves against the recorded worktree and reports no finding, while promotion resolves against its repository fallback, maps the declaration to no root and reports the changed path stray. After: both resolve against the recorded worktree and both report no finding. A footnote records that restoring the fallback reddens six new cases and leaves twenty green.">
      <figcaption>One declaration, one run, two trees. The recorded worktree path survives on the
      pointer after the directory is removed, and it is the path the grant was written
      against.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>The gate is a table the two surfaces must agree on, built for the
    disagreement.</strong> <code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code> gains a run whose
    recorded worktree is absent and, against it: a path under a declared directory accepted at
    both surfaces, a path under no declaration rejected at both, an intact worktree unaffected,
    and a table of six reclaimed-worktree pairs asserted equal between the two. The file is
    <strong>26 passed</strong> at the head, and the four-file cohort is <strong>1 failed, 68
    passed</strong> against <strong>1 failed, 59 passed</strong> at base <code>34f7f5f3</code>
    — the same single failure id at both revisions, and the nine-case difference is exactly this
    node's added cases.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base <code>34f7f5f3</code></th><th>Head</th><th>Delta</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Focused gate (<code>tests/test_write_scope_agreement.py</code>)</td><td>17 passed</td><td>26 passed</td><td>nine added, no removals</td></tr>

        <tr><td>Declared mutation (promotion resolves against the fallback tree)</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>6 failed, 20 passed</td><td>the six reclaimed cases, existing cases stay green</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p><strong>Falsified rather than asserted.</strong> The declared mutation restores the
    fallback at the promotion surface and reddens exactly six cases — the five declared
    reclaimed pairs and the named acceptance case — while the pre-existing seventeen and the new
    undeclared-path rejection and intact-worktree cases stay green, because a rejection is not
    flipped by removing a resolution step. The mutation is stated verbatim on the first line of
    <code>negative_control.log</code>, and the source was restored byte-identically afterwards
    (empty diff).</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change
    against its own base; the merged result at the integrated head is a separately dispatched
    test node's measurement, and the one cohort failure is pre-existing at base and outside this
    node's declared scope.</p>

  <section id="verify-gate-command-selection">
    <h2>verify-gate can be given the command it runs</h2>

    <p><strong>The gap was half a capability, and the missing half is now an
    option.</strong> <code>crew verify-gate</code> re-ran a run&#8217;s own recorded gate
    command at the integrated revision; it had no way to run something
    <em>wider</em>, so the fleet-wide suite the hand-check needed could not be
    measured through it. The verb now declares <code>--command</code>, which runs the
    given command in place of the stored one &#8212; and on a run that stored no gate
    command at all &#8212; against the tree at <code>--checkout-path</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>Where the selection happens.</strong> At the read from the run&#8217;s
    committed ledger row, <code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code>:
    <code>command = supplied or stored_command</code>. The supplied command wins
    only when it is non-empty, so the absent option is inert and every existing
    caller is unchanged. The re-run report carries <code>gate_command</code> &#8212; the
    command that actually executed, not the stored one &#8212; and
    <code>gate_command_source</code>, <code>option</code> or <code>stored</code>.</p>

    <figure>
      <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/verify-gate-command-selection.svg"
           alt="A decision chain from a verify-gate call: if --command is supplied and non-empty the supplied command runs with source option; otherwise the run's stored gate command runs with source stored; if neither is
           present the re-run is not-run with the reason that no gate command is stored.">
      <figcaption>One selection point, three outcomes, and the source recorded beside
      the command that ran.</figcaption>
    </figure>

    <p><strong>Four behaviours asserted, each entering through the CLI runner</strong>
    (<code>tests/test_verify_gate_command_option.py</code>): a supplied command runs in
    place of the stored one and the report records its source; without the option the
    stored command runs exactly as before; the report recorded on the run&#8217;s committed
    row names the command that executed rather than the stored one when they differ; and
    a supplied command on a run with no stored gate command is accepted rather than
    refused for the absence. Execution is proved by a marker each command writes, so the
    field&#8217;s own value is not the evidence that a command ran.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base <code>37de7907</code></th><th>Head</th><th>Delta</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>New option gate</td><td>file absent</td><td>4 passed</td><td>four cases, all green</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Focused cohort (three pre-existing files)</td><td>5 failed, 102 passed</td><td>5 failed, 106 passed</td><td>same five failure ids, four added passes</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Declared mutation (supplied command ignored)</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>3 failed, 1 passed</td><td>exactly the three option-dependent cases</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <p><strong>Falsified rather than asserted.</strong> The declared mutation replaces
    the selection with <code>command = stored_command</code>, so a supplied
    <code>--command</code> is ignored and the stored command runs instead. It reddens
    exactly <code>test_verify_gate_runs_the_supplied_command_not_the_stored_one</code>,
    <code>test_recorded_report_names_the_command_that_actually_ran</code> and
    <code>test_verify_gate_accepts_a_command_on_a_run_with_no_stored_gate</code>, while
    <code>test_verify_gate_without_the_option_runs_the_stored_command</code> stays green
    because it never supplies a command. <code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code> was restored
    byte-identically afterwards (empty diff), and the selection line reads
    <code>command = supplied or stored_command</code> again at <code>promotion.py:672</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>Correction to this anchor&#8217;s own receipt, recorded rather than
    removed.</strong> This anchor first stated that the mutation was &#8220;stated verbatim on
    the first line of <code>negative-control.log</code>&#8221;, and that was false when
    written. The declared mutation had never been supplied to this node: its dispatch carried
    the required first-line property but not the string to place there, and the log&#8217;s
    first line held only the node&#8217;s own code-level restatement of the edit. Promotion
    matches the first line against the declared string, so the receipt would have been refused
    for naming a property it did not hold. The declaration was recovered from the node&#8217;s
    review &#8212; traced to <code>compose_prompt</code> withholding it, found independently
    on another node, and landed as section 11 of this plan &#8212; and the log&#8217;s first
    line now carries it verbatim: <em>&#8220;ignore the supplied command so verify-gate
    falls back to the stored gate_check command whenever the row carries one&#8221;</em>.
    The code-level restatement is retained beneath it, because it names the exact edit the
    red run exercised and the declaration alone does not. The mutation, the three reddened
    cases and the byte-identical restore above are unchanged; only the receipt&#8217;s stated
    property was wrong.</p>

    <p><strong>Zero added failure ids at the base.</strong> The same three pre-existing
    cohort files ran at base <code>37de7907</code> in a detached worktree and reported the
    same five failure ids: <code>test_promotion_audits_only_its_own_worktree</code>, two
    crew-verb documentation contracts and two <code>reckon-ship</code> skill-budget
    contracts. None touches <code>verify-gate</code> or the option, and all five are
    pre-existing at base.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> This gate measures this node&#8217;s own
    change against its own base; the merged result at the integrated head is a separately
    dispatched test node&#8217;s measurement. The section&#8217;s second half &#8212; comparing a
    suite run against a fresh baseline worktree &#8212; is deliberately not built here: the
    reusable parts already exist as the ledger&#8217;s suite-observation and
    new-failure-set-difference helpers, which promotion consumes through
    <code>baseline_suite</code> and <code>after_suite</code>.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/cli.py</code>,
    <code>reckon/crew/promotion.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_verify_gate_command_option.py</code>,
    <code>docs/plans/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator.html</code>,
