  <section id="the-prompt-carries-the-declaration-it-demands">
    <h2>&sect;11 &mdash; The prompt carries the declaration it demands be repeated</h2>
    <p><strong>Defect reproduced before repair.</strong> The manifest template states that the red log's
    first line repeats the declared mutation verbatim, and promotion enforces it with a substring test
    (<code>if declaration not in text</code>), while the declaration itself reached no worker.
    Composing a prompt for a node whose <code>negative_control</code> reads
    <em>remove the interpolation so compose_prompt emits the requirement</em> returned the declaration
    <strong>0</strong> times in 9,989 prompt characters: every mention of the field in the composer was
    template text <em>about</em> it. The repair returns <strong>1</strong> occurrence in 10,490
    characters. Receipts: <code>repro-declaration-absent-base.log</code> and
    <code>after-declaration-present.log</code> in this node's run directory.</p>

    <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/declaration-reaches-the-worker.svg"
         alt="Two composed prompts side by side. Before: the manifest template states the requirement that the red log's first line repeat the declared mutation verbatim, while the declaration is interpolated nowhere, so the worker paraphrases and promotion refuses. After: a block above the manifest template carries the node's declaration as written, beside the requirement it answers, so the worker copies the string and the match holds. A strip beneath separates the three shapes the block keeps apart: a declared mutation, a declaration of no applicable mutation, and no declaration at all." />

    <p>Commits <code>fb68d3fb</code> and <code>8ba6c011</code> render the node's declaration through
    <code>_negative_control_declaration</code> in <code>reckon/crew/prompts.py</code> and interpolates the
    result immediately before the manifest template that holds the requirement. The declaration is
    inserted as written and never reflowed or re-indented, because it is the exact string the delivered
    log's first line must contain. Three shapes are kept apart rather than collapsed: a declared mutation
    states the string and requires the log; a declaration of <code>none: &lt;reason&gt;</code> states that
    no mutation applies, that its reason is expected after <code>none:</code>, and that promotion refuses a
    bare one for a node that writes a check, so no red log is required and the reason stands for a later
    reader; and a node that declares nothing keeps the subject and is told plainly that none was declared,
    because an omitted subject reads as a requirement that does not apply.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base</th><th>Landed</th><th>Delta</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>Declaration occurrences in a composed prompt</td><td>0 in 9,989 chars</td><td>1 in 10,490 chars</td><td>the string the red log must repeat is now in the prompt</td></tr>
        <tr><td><code>tests/test_prompt_carries_the_declaration.py</code></td><td>file absent</td><td>6 passed at the landed revision</td><td>6 new cases, all entering through <code>compose_prompt</code></td></tr>
        <tr><td>Focused cohort (15 files, prompts and negative-control surface)</td><td>8 failed, 158 passed at <code>37de7907</code></td><td>8 failed, 164 passed at <code>8ba6c011</code></td><td>0 added failure IDs (same 8 names)</td></tr>
        <tr><td>The declared control applied</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>6 failed, 0 passed</td><td>every case in the file reddens under it</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Ruff and <code>ruff format --check</code>, both changed paths</td><td><code>prompts.py</code>: 2 findings</td><td><code>prompts.py</code>: 2 findings; new test: 0</td><td>the commit adds none</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <h3>Falsified before trusting it</h3>
    <p><strong>The declared control is the interpolation itself</strong>, and it is the declaration the
    run's <code>node.negative_control</code> carries: <em>remove the interpolation so compose_prompt
    emits the requirement about repeating the declared mutation without ever including the declaration
    itself</em>. Realised by deleting the <code>{negative_control_declaration}</code> placeholder from the
    prompt template, the file reddens <strong>6 failed</strong> &mdash; all six cases, including the one
    that reads the block alone and the one carrying quotes and newlines. The declaration is the red log's
    first line verbatim, so the log is a substring match for the declaration promotion would test, and
    <code>reckon/crew/prompts.py</code> was restored afterwards with the file green again at the same
    revision. Logs: <code>negative-control-red.log</code>, <code>green-restored.log</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>One sentence corrected on review, because it claimed a check that does not exist</strong>
    (commit <code>8ba6c011</code>). The <code>none:</code> branch originally told the worker that the node
    <em>states</em> the reason, but nothing enforces that when the node is cut: a bare <code>none</code>
    passes the dispatch check and is refused only once promotion discharges the declaration on a passing
    gate. A prompt asserting a property of its own node that no check upholds is the substitution this
    contract exists to remove, so the sentence now states the condition that actually holds. Enforcing the
    reason at dispatch &mdash; the better repair &mdash; is outside this node's fence and is carried as a
    follow-on against <code>reckon/crew/node.py</code>. This correction also restates two log labels that
    named the base revision while the tree already held the repair: <code>green-focused.log</code> and
    <code>green-restored.log</code> now name the revision they ran at, and the cohort, the red control and
    the green re-run are all taken at <code>8ba6c011</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change against its
    own base at <code>37de7907</code>; the merged head is a separately dispatched test node's
    measurement. The focused cohort carries eight failures, present identically at base, and they are
    outside this node's declared scope. Two of them sit on this plan's surface and are reported as
    follow-ons rather than repaired here: the manifest template's <code>negative_control_log</code> gloss
    is asserted to contain the phrase <em>red log</em> while it says <em>the log's first line</em>, and
    the refusal case in the header test meets promotion's unreviewed-run classification before it reaches
    the negative-control refusal.</p>

    <p>Changed paths: <code>reckon/crew/prompts.py</code>,
    <code>tests/test_prompt_carries_the_declaration.py</code>,
    <code>docs/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/declaration-reaches-the-worker.svg</code>,
    <code>docs/plans/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator.html</code>. Base revision
    <code>37de7907</code>; commits <code>fb68d3fb</code> and <code>8ba6c011</code>. Receipts:
    <code>~/.config/reckon/crew/runs/r-20260922T171701435432-the-prompt-carries-the-declaration-it-demands/</code>
    (<code>green-focused.log</code>, <code>base-focused.log</code>,
    <code>negative-control-red.log</code>, <code>green-restored.log</code>,
    <code>repro-declaration-absent-base.log</code>, <code>after-declaration-present.log</code>).</p>
  </section>

  <section id="the-declared-branch-matches-the-promotion-predicate">
    <h2>&sect;11 &mdash; The declared-control branch is selected on the predicate promotion applies</h2>
    <p><strong>Defect reproduced before repair.</strong> The block above the manifest template told every
    node whose declaration was not a <code>none</code> form that <em>this node writes a check</em> and asked
    it to name a red log's path. Promotion discharges a declared mutation only for a node whose write paths
    reach a test path and exempts any other with <code>node-writes-no-test-path</code> before it reads the
    declaration, so a node that declared a mutation and wrote no test file was told a false property of its
    own node and sent to produce a red log nothing consumes. Composing a prompt for a node declaring
    <em>removing the guard turns this case red</em> with <code>write_paths</code> holding only a source
    path returned <em>This node writes a check</em> <strong>present</strong> and <em>Name that log's path in
    the <code>negative_control_log</code> line</em> <strong>present</strong>, while promotion's own
    predicate reports no test path for that node. Receipt:
    <code>repro-noncheck-node.log</code> in this node's run directory.</p>

    <img src="/reckon/figures/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator/prompt-branch-matches-the-promotion-predicate.svg"
         alt="A declaration forks first on its shape — empty, a none form, or a mutation — reproducing the undeclared and none branches unchanged. A mutation then forks on the predicate promotion applies, any(is_test_path(p)): yes selects the declared rule that states the node writes a check and asks for the red log's path; no selects the repaired declared-no-check rule that states the declaration is recorded and no log is required. A footnote records that before the repair the branch was chosen on the declaration form alone, so the no branch was never reached." />

    <p><strong>The repair selects the branch on the predicate, not on the declaration form alone.</strong>
    <code>_negative_control_declaration</code> in <code>reckon/crew/prompts.py</code> now picks its rule by
    <code>negative_control_is_none</code> first (the branch the none form has always taken), then by
    <code>any(is_test_path(path) for path in node.write_paths)</code> &mdash; the same predicate promotion
    evaluates before it reads the declaration. A node whose scope reaches a test path keeps the requirement
    and the log-path instruction; a node whose scope reaches none is told the declaration is recorded and
    that no log is required, and the declaration string is still rendered because it is the node's record.
    The <code>none</code> and undeclared branches are unchanged.</p>

    <table>
      <thead><tr><th>Measure</th><th>Base <code>d53318f6</code></th><th>Landed <code>77d8196c</code></th><th>Delta</th></tr></thead>
      <tbody>
        <tr><td>A prompt for a declared mutation with no test path &mdash; claims a check, and asks for a log</td><td>claim <strong>present</strong>, log-path ask <strong>present</strong></td><td>claim <strong>absent</strong>, log-path ask <strong>absent</strong>, declaration still present</td><td>the prompt no longer states a false property of the node</td></tr>
        <tr><td>A prompt for a declared mutation with a test path &mdash; claim and log-path ask</td><td>both present</td><td>both present</td><td>the requirement the check-writing node needs is untouched</td></tr>
        <tr><td><code>tests/test_prompt_carries_the_declaration.py</code></td><td>6 cases</td><td>10 passed</td><td>4 new cases, all entering through <code>compose_prompt</code></td></tr>
        <tr><td>Focused cohort (15 files, prompts and negative-control surface)</td><td>8 failed, 164 passed</td><td>8 failed, 168 passed</td><td>0 added failure IDs (identical 8 names, diffed)</td></tr>
        <tr><td>The declared control applied</td><td>&mdash;</td><td>1 failed, 9 passed</td><td>the no-test-path case reddens; the other three new cases stay green</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Ruff and <code>ruff format --check</code>, both changed paths</td><td><code>prompts.py</code>: 2 findings</td><td><code>prompts.py</code>: 2 findings; new cases: 0</td><td>the commit adds none</td></tr>
      </tbody>
    </table>

    <h3>Falsified before trusting it</h3>
    <p><strong>The declared control is the branch selector itself</strong>, and it is the declaration the
    run's <code>node.negative_control</code> carries: <em>select the declared-control branch on whether the
    declaration is a none form alone, ignoring whether the node write paths reach a test path</em>. Realised
    by reverting the selector to <code>NONE_RULE if none else DECLARED_RULE</code>, the file reddens
    <strong>1 failed, 9 passed</strong>: the new no-test-path case fails because the prompt again asserts the
    node writes a check, while the three other new cases hold. That is the sharpest form of the control
    &mdash; the mutation is exactly the defect, so the case written for it is the only one that moves, and a
    case that reddened for any other reason could not be it. The mutation is the red log's first line
    verbatim, and <code>reckon/crew/prompts.py</code> was restored afterwards with <code>git diff</code>
    empty at that path and the file re-run green at the same revision. Logs:
    <code>negative-control-red.log</code>, <code>green-restored.log</code>.</p>

    <p><strong>What this does not claim.</strong> The gate measures this node's own change against its base
    at <code>d53318f6</code>; the merged head is a separately dispatched test node's measurement. The
    focused cohort carries eight failures, present identically at base and diffed to be the same names, and
    they are outside this node's declared scope. One is on this plan's surface and is reported as a
    follow-on rather than repaired here: <code>test_crew_negative_control_header.py</code>'s refusal case
    meets promotion's unreviewed-run classification before it reaches the negative-control refusal.
    <code>reckon/crew/node.py</code> was in this node's fence and is untouched, because
    <code>is_test_path</code> was already importable and enforcing the <code>none: reason</code> form at
    dispatch remains a separate follow-on.</p>

    <code>docs/evidence/archive/work-does-not-fall-to-the-coordinator-landed.html</code>,

unknown · undated

A node that blocks at its fence cannot be widened by any command. Measured on this plan's own §2 node. the-roster-write-commits-itself reproduced the defect, committed a durable failing regression, measured the obvious repair and refuted it — a commit inside ledger.register_member adds three failures, because internal session-owned registrations call it too — and then blocked correctly, because the narrow repair needs reckon/cli.py to distinguish the public member add from those internal callers, and cli.py was outside the fence I gave it. The fence was my error, not the node's.

There is no command that repairs it. --write-path appears on dispatch alone; crew resume takes --run, --advice, --backend, --reason and nothing else. So the two exits are to hand-edit write_paths in the live pointer, or to redispatch and discard the worker's session with every turn of context it holds. crew complete --accept-path PATH REASON exists and resolves the promotion of an undeclared companion path — the downstream half of the same problem — but it reaches the coordinator after the fact and does nothing for the worker, who has already stopped and asked.

This is the second measured instance beside c-no-command-narrows-a-claim, and together they say the same thing: a claim can be created at dispatch and can be destroyed at promotion, and cannot be changed in between. Every mid-flight scope movement therefore lands on the coordinator as a hand edit to shared state.

unknown · undated

A review run cannot be reconciled through the surface at all, so every wave that reviews its own nodes leaves permanent unreconciled runs behind it. Measured today on four of them.

A review worker delivers a parsed review JSON under ~/.config/reckon/crew/reviews/<project>/ and writes that path, with its scratch files, into the manifest's changed_paths. Its commits field is correctly empty — there is nothing to commit, because none of those paths is inside a repository. crew complete then refuses: "has a complete manifest with changed_paths, but the manifest field 'commits' is missing. Promotion cannot verify changed repository paths without the commit that contains them". --no-commit does not reach it, because the refusal is about the paths rather than the absent commit. crew drain --leave accepts only handed-off or still-working, and neither is true of a run that finished and delivered.

The consequence is mechanical. Those four runs sit unreconciled forever, the 5-minute grace expires, and every subsequent dispatch in the project is refused until it carries --allow-unreconciled-runs — which this session then had to pass to dispatch the next node. The waiver is designed for a judgement call and is being spent on a run that has no other disposition available. Note that the surface already knows non-repository delivery directories are legitimate exclusive scopes; tests/test_crew.py::test_non_repository_delivery_directories_are_valid_exclusive_scopes asserts it for review_store_root. Promotion's changed_paths check is the one place that does not.

unknown · undated

Dispatch takes upwards of a minute because resolving scope claims recomposes the whole project state once per live pointer. Measured by cProfile on a --dry-run dispatch — no worktree, no process, no record — which still cost 48 seconds of wall clock against 9 seconds of CPU, so the time is waiting rather than computing.

FrameCallsCumulative
_repository_scope_claims167.2s of 73.5s
_resolved_scope_entries4362.7s
runs.py _project_derivations31 — one per live pointer61.4s
compose_project_state3161.3s
_hydrate_items3159.7s
_plan_html.read_state285 plan documents28.2s
_io.open15,31516.1s

The disproportion is the finding. _project_derivations wants one small derivations mapping, which lives in the composed project manifest; to get it, it composes the entire project — sprints, milestones, blockers, timeline — and _hydrate_items parses every plan HTML in that project. The claim walk then does this once per live claim. Across five mounted repositories one dispatch parsed 285 plan documents.

Memoising the lookup alone takes 31 compositions to 5 and roughly halves wall clock, measured as a paired A/B run twice: 28.1s to 17.6s, then 43.4s to 22.8s. The spread between the two unmemoised runs is GPFS page-cache noise, which is why the comparison is paired rather than absolute — and why the repair's measure counts plan parses rather than seconds. Dispatched as dispatch-does-not-recompose-the-project-per-claim, whose falsifier requires the parse count to be independent of the number of live pointers.

One methodological note, because it cost a wrong reading first: from reckon.crew import dispatch binds the dispatch function rather than the submodule, so a monkeypatch through that name silently does nothing while the run still looks like evidence. The first A/B reported a 48s to 27s improvement from a patch that never executed; the real cause was page-cache warming.

unknown · undated

The imas-ambix coordinator reported eleven crew and dispatch rough edges measured while shipping an unrelated node, and two of them are this session's own findings arrived at independently, which is what makes them worth acting on rather than filing.

  • Their #3 is c-a-review-run-cannot-be-reconciled from the other side. They hit it in the investigate role, this session in the review role, in different projects: a run whose declared deliverable lies outside any repository lists it in changed_paths with an empty commits, and promotion refuses for want of a commit that cannot exist. Their only route was to hand-edit the worker's manifest to changed_paths: []. Two roles, two projects, one cause.
  • Their #9 is the review-of-review recursion, also seen here four times: next_action prefixes review-of- unconditionally without checking whether the run it describes is already a review, so the suggestion for a review run is to review the review.
  • Their #1 is the serious one and does not yet reproduce from source. They report that a crew complete refused for a misspelled verdict had already appended the run's comment to the plan, and that the partial write then blocked the retry with a different error about uncommitted changes outside the worktree. Reading promotion.py, the verdict guard is the first statement of promote_run (:1854) and the comment is composed far later (:2607, :2785), so that function cannot do what they observed — which means a second path, not a misreading on their part. Their exact argv will settle which, and it is worth settling: a validation failure that leaves a partial write behind is the same family as plan writes are not fenced.
  • Their #8 is the cheapest high-value item in either set. A declared wait carries a success terminal (wait_terminal) and no failure terminal, so when the probe's subject ceases to exist the empty result reads as “not yet” rather than “never” and the wait becomes an unbounded poll against something already dead. A failure predicate collapses their #7 and #11 as well, because a worker that can report does not need a channel to be steered through — and no channel exists, since a print-mode worker has no turn after its last one.
  • Their #10 is the complement of a node in flight here. an-undeterminable-status-is-still-refused is repairing the reader so a status carrying no determinable word refuses rather than parsing to an empty string; their case is that an off-vocabulary status renders the run permanently unreadable, indistinguishable from a corrupt manifest, while the work itself is merged and correct. One seam, and better repaired once.
  • Their #2 (--gate pass rejected where the skill's own prose says “gate pass”), #4 (a metered-default-backend refusal classified as an eight-property contract defect, aiming the reader at the node rather than the lane), #5 (auto member selection reassigning a member it just assigned in the same turn, refusing as member-in-flight) and #6 (crew resume naming two remedies, observe and stop, of which only stop clears the condition) are each a message or a classification pointing the reader away from the remedy.

The wait-design question their #7, #8 and #11 share is theirs to own and belongs in its own plan; the rest are this plan's subject exactly — a surface whose gaps are paid for by hand, one coordinator at a time.

unknown · undated

The imas-ambix report's #1 resolved, across two sessions, into two defects that are both worse than the one originally reported — and the original reading, that a refused promotion writes the plan, is withdrawn. All three of their refusals are accounted for at guards far upstream of any write: the verdict guard is the first statement of complete (promotion.py:1854), _require_gate_evidence follows at :1881, and both _record_landing_comment calls sit at :2610 and :2788 inside _complete_locked, which begins at :2542. No refusal reaches a plan write.

The landing comment is attributed to someone who did not write it. Both call sites pass author=str(record.get("member") or record.get("role") or "reckon") — the run's roster member. The coordinator performs this write; the comment records the worker. Their case carried data-who="session-2aaa9a83a210fa1b777d", an ephemeral auto-registered member, on a comment their worker provably never wrote: a git log -S on the comment id returned exactly one commit, the coordinator's own, and the worker's landing commit contained zero occurrences of that id and no comments section at all. This is unconditional, so every coordinator-written landing comment on every project carries a who that names the wrong party.

The idempotency check compares identity, never content. At :1397 the writer returns recorded: True, already_recorded: True on finding any comment whose id matches, without looking at its body. The id is derived from the run id alone. So a promotion re-run with a corrected outcome finds the old comment, reports success, and changes nothing — the plan keeps the superseded text and the caller is told it landed. In their case the two strings happened to coincide, which is the only reason the plan ended up correct; nothing in the mechanism arranged that.

Their proposed check is the right one and is cheap: already_recorded: true should only be returned when the matching comment is reachable from a commit the worker authored, which is what _worker_authored_landing_record already computes and which the id-match branch short-circuits past. The attribution repair is separate and simpler — record the writer.

Worth keeping the shape rather than only the two bugs: the reported symptom was a partial write on a refusal, the measured causes were a wrong name and a silent no-op, and reordering the write — the obvious repair from the symptom — would have fixed neither while appearing to close the report.

unknown · undated

Measured at the repair, before merging it. The same --dry-run dispatch, run alternately against the main checkout and the node's worktree, with a counter on _plan_html.read_state:

TreeWallPlan documents parsed
main77.4s286
repair19.6s0
main96.3s285
repair24.7s0

Roughly a fourfold improvement in wall clock, and the mechanism is gone rather than reduced: zero plan documents parsed, twice. That is the measure the node was given, and it is the right one precisely because it is insensitive to the noise that makes the seconds column untrustworthy — the two unrepaired runs differ by 19 seconds while their parse counts differ by one.

The repair is the deep one rather than the memo this plan's first comment proposed. project_state.read_project_derivations reads only the authored project manifest — the distributed resource, or the legacy index row when the project has not migrated — so nothing hydrates and the composition never happens. That handles both project-state formats, which matters because one of the thirteen mounted projects is still legacy.

The residual 20 to 25 seconds is not this defect and is not yet attributed. Worth saying plainly so the next reader does not assume dispatch is now fast: it is four times less slow.

unknown · undated

The unreconcilable review backlog grows by one for every node reviewed, which is the cost of c-a-review-run-cannot-be-reconciled expressed as a rate rather than as a defect.

Measured this morning at four. Measured six hours later, after one wave: seven of the nine scoring runs are review runs that cannot be promoted — their delivered paths lie outside any repository so promotion refuses for want of a commit, and drain --leave has no disposition describing a run that finished and delivered. The other two are implement runs legitimately waiting for a review, which is the state working as designed.

So every dispatch in this project now carries --allow-unreconciled-runs, and it is spent not on a judgement call but on a class of run that has no other exit. A waiver that is always required stops being a waiver: the next reader cannot tell which of the seven, if any, deserved attention. That is the same shape as a saturated gate measure — a signal that fires every time carries no information.

The §2 repair is confirmed working in a different project, under real load, within the hour it landed. unknown · undated

Six further rough edges from the imas-efit S13 coordinator, routed at the lead's request. Two are confirmed here first-hand, one does not reproduce, and one is a third independent report of an open decision on this plan.

  • The documented arming line is not runnable. Dispatch emits attach_line: "reckon crew follow --project <p> --session <s>" and the harness reference tells the coordinator to put exactly that string in a monitor. reckon is not on PATH on this workstation — it lives in the checkout's .venv/bin — so the monitor dies at once with exit 127. Confirmed here: this session has used the absolute interpreter path all day for exactly that reason. The compounding part is their observation that the next symptom is a watcher-required refusal on dispatch, which looks unrelated to a monitor that died a minute earlier. The emitted line should resolve from the running reckon, as worktree creation already does.
  • There is no cheap way to ask which members are free. member add makes the caller invent the id, so the caller must first learn the highest allocated one; member list is the only route and returns roughly 40 KB for 90 members, most of it JSON-encoded configuration keys used as dictionary keys, with no role filter and no in-flight filter — although dispatch computes exactly that predicate when it refuses member-in-flight. A read per wave, to obtain a fact the surface already knows.
  • A sprint-scoped ready set cannot be obtained in one call — but not for the reason reported. They concluded roadmap(project, sprint=…, view="summary") ignores sprint. Measured here, it does not: sprint="S18" returns 8 plans against 88 project-wide. What is true, and is the sharper finding, is that the summary returns counts rather than the ready set — "ready": 2, with no list — while view="raw" exceeds the transport ceiling on a real project. So the skill's instruction to use roadmap as the canonical plan-level graph still has no working call behind it for a dispatch queue, which is their point and it stands.
  • The five-minute unreconciled grace, reported for the third time by a third fleet. Their wording is the clearest yet: a legitimate refill is refused because the coordinator is doing the very work the fence exists to force. This is the unpromoted-grace-seconds decision on this plan, and three independent reports is enough evidence to lock it rather than collect a fourth.
  • Follower re-arming is a thirty-minute chore whose lapse is silent. The host caps a monitor at 30 minutes and dispatch requires a live follower, so the only signal that the follower lapsed is a watcher-required refusal on the next dispatch. Their previous session logged four such episodes in one night; this session has re-armed three times today. A refusal that reported how long ago the registration lapsed would make it self-diagnosing.
  • The single-goal refusal names the token but not the clause. Refined rather than confirmed: it does echo the offending conjunction — this session was refused today with "the goal joins deliverables with 'and'" — so their ask is narrower than stated, which is to name which clause tripped it on a long goal carrying several. Worth noting that the validator earned its keep in both cases: theirs was arguably one deliverable in two ordered steps, mine was genuinely two.

unknown · undated

Correction to c-the-landing-comment-misattributes-and-fails-quiet: the withdrawal recorded there was wrong. That comment states that the originally reported symptom — a refused promotion writing the plan — does not reproduce, because every refusal sits at a guard far upstream of both writes. A third fleet then reproduced it, and tracing the actual refusal shows the ordering is the other way round.

The guard found earlier, _require_gate_evidence at promotion.py:1881, is real and is upstream. It is not the guard that fires. The refusal comes from ledger.build_record, raising at ledger.py:1133, and the call order inside _complete_locked is:

promotion.py:2808   _record_landing_comment(…)   <- writes the comment, bumps plan-version
promotion.py:2858   ledger.build_record(…)       <- raises "a passing gate requires the
                                                 check that produced it"

Fifty lines. The plan is mutated before the check that rejects the call. The earlier reading was an unaimed check reported as an absence: a guard was located, it was upstream, and its being the wrong guard was never tested.

The cascade is why this outranks the single dirty file, measured live by the imas-efit S13 coordinator in three consecutive calls. A first crew complete missing the gate flags wrote the comment, moved plan-version from 8 to 9, then refused. The retry supplying those flags was refused by a different guard, for uncommitted changes at a declared path in the main checkout — dirty only because the first attempt dirtied it. A third attempt was refused because the comment already on disk carried a different narrative from the corrected outcome, so the machinery defended the first attempt's wording against its own author. It succeeded only when the outcome was passed byte-identical to what the refused call had written.

One operator mistake, three refusals wearing three different faces, and the third actively misleading — it reads as another agent having written the record, when the other agent was the same call five minutes earlier. The first malformed promotion also pins the outcome wording of every later attempt, which is a silent constraint no message mentions.

The repair is ordering, not new guards: validate before writing, since none of these checks needs the write to have happened. Dispatched as a-refused-promotion-writes-nothing, whose falsifier requires a refused promotion to leave the plan byte-identical including plan-version, and the immediately following retry to succeed with no intervening commit or cleanup.

Worth keeping about the shape of the error as well as the defect: two separate fleets reported this symptom, this session traced it to a guard, found that guard innocent, and generalised from one guard to all of them. The defects found along the way — the attribution and the body-blind idempotency check — were real and have landed, but they were adjacent defects in the same function, and finding them made the wrong conclusion feel corroborated.

Correction to followup f-cdocw-002, which records a-refused-promotion-writes-nothing as “dispatched but unlanded”. It was not dispatched. It had been refused on the codex budget hold at the same moment as two sibling dispatches, and the refusal was read through a parser printing only the ok flag, the error key and the run id — so the reason never reached the coordinator, who reported the node as in flight to the lead and wrote that into the followup.

That is the never pipe a refusal channel rule, broken by the session that had it written down: a crew command piped into a JSON reader turns a readable refusal into whatever the reader chose to print. Two of the three refusals recovered only because a later unpiped read showed the budget hold, its 92% utilisation and its reset date.

All three are now running on the local lane, which returned to service the same afternoon after a ten-minute outage with its device pool doubled: a-refused-promotion-writes-nothing, a-run-record-says-whether-it-resumed, and the review that will let the merged a-nested-key-stays-nested be promoted.

The followup's closing paragraph on lane availability is stale for the same reason lane state always is. Re-read it; do not trust a lane figure written into a plan.

unknown · undated

A peer fleet reported credible character-level output corruption on the local lane, in the window this session wrote five source files through it. Recording what was checked and what was not, because if the report firms up the next reader needs the boundary rather than the conclusion.

Their evidence, offered as two instances with no denominator: a committed except TypeError, ValueError: in one fleet, and in another limits = [int(x) …] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else [17, 32): — a closing bracket substituted by a parenthesis plus an inserted colon, two character errors in one line. The second is the stronger signal: the first is a generation mistake a model makes unprompted, while the second is not something anyone means to write. Corroborating, that node rewrote one file seven times and accumulated nine error results before stopping. They explicitly could not establish cause, trigger or scope, and synthetic probes came back byte-exact, so a short cold request does not reproduce it.

Checked here, in two passes. ast.parse on every changed Python file at each worktree head, source and tests — all parse. Then every added and removed source line read across five diffs: recovery.py +151, dispatch.py +41, promotion.py +45, reports.py +30, doccheck.py +27. No dropped bracket, no stray colon, no substituted operator, no reversed comparison, and the logic in each matches its brief.

What was not checked: the test files line by line, only parse-checked. Four are new files of 280 to 380 lines.

Why main is unexposed, and why that is luck. The only source commit merged since the window opened is a recovery — 127 lines of tests written two days earlier on a different lane. Every affected node's work is still in its worktree awaiting review. They are unmerged because reviews are pending, not because anyone anticipated this.

The verification point worth keeping regardless of whether the report holds. A syntax check is necessary and not sufficient: a substituted operator, digit or comparison direction compiles cleanly and passes a gate the same worker wrote. The only instrument that reaches it is reading the diff, which this project's own contract already requires and which is easy to treat as a formality when a node reports green. Five files and roughly 290 lines read by one reader who knew what to look for is a weak instrument, and it was reported to the peer as one clean datum rather than as an exoneration.

reckon-ship · undated

Critical, reported by a peer after it forced a hand edit of reckon-owned state, and verified here rather than taken. docs/.reckon/project-state-migration.json presents as a completed-migration audit record — it carries status, completed_at, source_sha256, snapshot_sha256 and parity_sha256. It is in fact the live resource registry. project_state.py:1356 reads marker.get("resources") on every compose and raises distributed marker has no resource inventory when it is absent, then iterates those rows to decide which resources exist. The directory glob only adds to that set; the registry decides it.

Why the naming is the defect rather than a detail. The constant is MARKER_RELATIVE = Path(".reckon/project-state-migration.json"), and a file called a migration marker, carrying a completion timestamp and three parity hashes, reads as something you falsify by touching. The peer nearly did not edit it for exactly that reason. A file that governs every read should not be shaped like a record of something that already finished.

One of the peer's worries is unfounded and worth retiring. They feared their edit now sits beside hashes describing the pre-edit inventory, arming a delayed failure. Checked with a positive control, because an absence and an unaimed grep return the same empty result: parity_sha256 appears in the marker data on this project, and appears nowhere in reckon's source at all. Nothing reads the hashes back, so nothing validates resources against them and no delayed failure is armed. The hashes are inert provenance.

What it cost, which is the reason this belongs on this plan. A blocker cannot be retired through any vocabulary the surface exposes. The blocker schema has no status field. Setting archived through the resource API is accepted, bumps the version, and is inert, because archived is derived from the file path rather than stored. Moving the file to archive/ breaks the project read outright with an error naming the file rather than the registry. Only a hand edit of the registry works — outside expected_version, outside the 412, outside schema validation and outside any audit. Two coordinators doing that concurrently lose one another's rows and nothing reports it. That is coordinator hand-work created by a missing field, which is this plan's subject exactly.

Preference order for the repair, the peer's and endorsed here. A status field on the blocker resource with the composition filtering on it, which is cheapest and keeps the record readable rather than deleting it. Failing that, a retire operation owning both halves — registry row and file — so no coordinator touches the registry directly. And independently of either, split the registry out of the file carrying the migration hashes, because the name is doing real harm.

State left behind on that project, reversible from git at ec425b11: seven blocker files under docs/blockers/archive/, seven rows absent from marker.resources, composed blockers sixteen to nine, roadmap reporting no findings.
